The 0.22 point between the first two is the filter; the further 0.95 is *not* the filter but a louder halo waking voices `ResonanceBus::is_active` used to let sleep — which is the feature working, and the number to watch when a preset voices the bridge hard. Neutrality was verified by rendering, not by reasoning, as item 103 requires: `piano-emulator render demo` and `render` (the compass sweep) built before and after this change are byte-identical WAVs (md5 `94d9d076…` and `ad120191…`), and so is the demo rendered through `presets/salamander-c5.toml` (`fac48d3c…`) — three renders, two presets, both of which leave `[voicing.bridge]` out. `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk and `preset::tests::neutral_refinements_are_absent_from_the_file_and_read_back_neutral` now names `voicing.bridge` alongside the other four fields a neutral preset must not mention.

153. **The duplex segments are a bank beside the string, not a second bus, and their two drives are the two things a bridge carries.** `PHYSICS.md` §3 asks for a per-key `ModalBank` "in `PianoString` beside the polarizations", driven by the key's own bridge force *plus* the sympathetic bus. It is a module of its own (`engine/src/duplex.rs`, `DuplexBank`) held by `Voice` rather than by `PianoString`, for one reason: everything in `string.rs` is damped and these are the one part of the instrument that never is, and putting an undamped bank inside the object whose whole API is `set_damper`/`damper_profile`/`reset` would have made every future damper change a place to get this wrong. The drive is assembled in `Voice::process` from the two signals that are already there — `out`, the key's mono sum for this block *after* the felt limiter (the limiter acts on the bridge force, and the bridge is exactly what the segments hang off), and the block `ResonanceBus::drive` produced, i.e. `coupling · (B(bus) − own_gain · own_previous)`. The second one is not a new path: it is the same buffer the strings are given, computed once and handed to both, which is why this work had to come after the admittance (item 148). One consequence is worth stating: because the segments join the voice's mono sum, they reach the board at the key's pan *and* they feed the bus, so one key's duplex can drive another's. Their own contribution comes back off the bus through the same `own_gain` subtraction as the strings'.

154. **`gain_db` is the segment's response at its own frequency, so that level and length are separate parameters — which they have to be, because they are separate measurements.** A modal resonator's raw input gain is not a level: at the same `g`, a mode with a 3 s decay answers a steady drive sixty times harder than one with a 0.05 s decay, so a preset that lengthened a segment would find it had also made it louder by 36 dB. `duplex::resonator` therefore realises `g = 2 G (1 − r)`, which puts the mode's steady response to a unit sinusoid at its own frequency on exactly `G = 10^(gain_db/20)` whatever `t60_s` is (`a_segment_answers_its_own_frequency_at_the_gain_the_preset_asked_for`, `the_level_of_a_segment_does_not_depend_on_how_long_it_rings`). The factor of two is the negative-frequency image, which contributes nothing at resonance. This is also what makes the loop bound of item 156 computable: `G` *is* the segment's contribution to any loop that closes at its frequency, and it is a number the file states rather than a number the sample rate and the decay conspire to produce.

155. **An undamped bank must not wake the strings it sits next to, so `is_idle` was split in two.** Nothing stops a duplex — not the key, not the sustain pedal, not sostenuto (which catches damper levers, and there is no lever), not una corda (which moves the hammer) — and `Voice::process` used one predicate to decide whether the whole voice ran. Left alone, 88 banks that never sleep would have kept 88 voices and their ~14 000 string partials running for the length of the piece. The predicate is now two: `Voice::strings_idle` gates the hammer, the damper ramp, the string banks and the felt limiter, while the segments run on their own test (still holding something, or something can drive them — this key's strings, or a bus with another key's note on it). `Voice::is_idle` remains the honest union of both, so `Engine::active_voices` still counts a key whose segments are ringing, and the branch that writes no samples at all is still taken when *both* are quiet: `an_idle_voice_with_silent_segments_renders_nothing_at_all` renders an untouched buffer and an untouched board from a preset with segments on the key. `ModalBank`'s culling does the rest — a segment that is not driven falls under `CULL_AMPLITUDE`, is zeroed, and the voice goes back to sleep (`ringing_segments_do_not_keep_the_strings_awake_and_go_quiet_by_themselves`, `a_segment_left_alone_decays_to_exact_silence`). `MAX_DUPLEX_T60_S` is 3 s for the same reason, and `PHYSICS.md` §3's "shorter T60 than intuition suggests" is that reason.

156. **The loop 88 permanently undamped banks can close is bounded from their realised response, and what the bound refuses is the preset that tunes them all alike.** This is a different loop from the bridge's (item 149) and a worse one: a string's contribution to the coupling loop dies with the note, and a segment's does not, so a loop gain of 0.99 that would be inaudible on strings has forever to grow here. `Preset::validate_duplex` measures it. Segment `j` puts `D_j(f)` on the bus per unit of drive — the realised sum of its modes' magnitudes, `duplex::magnitude`, not the numbers in the file — and gets back `coupling · B(f)` of every other segment's output and `coupling · (B(f) − own_gain_j)` of its own, so the tightest loop at any frequency is `coupling · max|B| · (Σ_j |D_j(f)| + max_j |D_j(f)|)`, evaluated at every segment's own centre (where a sum of resonances peaks) and held under `MAX_DUPLEX_LOOP_GAIN = 0.25` — a quarter of unity, the margin item 149 uses. The sum is what carries the meaning: a preset that puts every key's segments on one frequency is 88 undamped resonators in one loop and is refused, while the same segments scattered by a quarter of a semitone per key pass, because a segment with a 1.5 s decay has a bandwidth under a hertz and two of them tens of cents apart do not answer for each other at all (`a_duplex_tuned_to_one_frequency_across_the_compass_is_refused`). That is not a compromise with the physics but a statement of it: Öberg & Askenfelt's finding is precisely that real duplex tuning *scatters*, by 25 cents within one trichord and by up to 190 across keys, and a layout that does not scatter is not a piano. A measured layout sits two orders of magnitude inside the bound (`a_voiced_duplex_round_trips_and_stays_far_inside_the_loop_bound`). `DRIVE_CEILING` remains the unconditional backstop under all of it.

157. **A segment only speaks where the drive has energy, which in this engine means on a partial or off the bus — measured, because it decides how the field will be estimated.** One string's bridge force is a sum of decaying sinusoids at its own partials, so a resonator with a bandwidth under a hertz sees almost nothing between them. Struck C6, one segment at +6 dB and a 1.5 s decay, energy in the bank at the end of the strike: on partial 3 exactly, `3.8e−8`; two cents off, 0.9 dB down; six cents, 5.6 dB; fifteen cents, 15.3 dB; fifty cents, 23.7 dB — by which point it is indistinguishable from a segment sitting between the partials entirely (24.6 dB down). Three things follow. (a) The aliquot placement is not decoration: a rear duplex nominally tuned to a partial is *why* the segment sounds, and `PHYSICS.md` §3's warning against writing `k·f0` is about the cents, not about the octave. (b) A fitted `hz` is therefore worth cents of accuracy and a fitted `gain_db` is nearly meaningless without it, which is a real constraint on the estimator sketched in §3. (c) The other path is the bus, where the drive is the whole instrument and has energy everywhere — which is the half of the feature that answers other keys, and the half that will get louder when stage 2 raises the coupling. The levels the model reaches: a staccato C6 leaves its segments 61 dB under the note's peak half a second after the key came up (an RMS against a peak, half a second into a 1.5 s decay), against `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5's `harm*` measurements of −31 dB at C3 and −39 dB at C5 ringing 1–2 s. Same neighbourhood, from one string's own bridge force, at the top of the schema's range.

158. **What the segments cost, and the proof that a preset without them is untouched.** `SPEC.md`'s worst case (sustain down, an 88-key glissando left ringing, best of three 10 s renders, M4 Pro): **38.8 %** of one core with no segments, **40.4 %** with six on every key — 528 resonators that no damper can stop — for **+1.6 points**, against the 50 % goal. The case the waking would have shown up in worst is one held note with every other key damped, where the bus reaches 87 voices' segments and nothing else: **1.87 % → 2.44 %**, which is the split of item 155 doing its job (without it, one note would have cost what a glissando does). Both are asserted, not merely printed, in `the_worst_case_with_a_duplex_on_every_key_fits_the_performance_budget`. Neutrality was verified by rendering, as item 103 requires: `piano-emulator render demo`, `render` (the compass sweep) and the demo through `presets/salamander-c5.toml` are byte-identical to the renders taken before this change (md5 `94d9d076…`, `ad120191…`, `fac48d3c…` — the same three hashes item 152 records, since neither preset has a `notes.duplex` table). `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk, and `neutral_refinements_are_absent_from_the_file_and_read_back_neutral` now names `duplex` beside the other five. **The tuner's copy of the schema is still not written** — item 148's caveat, now covering `DuplexMode` and its bounds as well as the bridge's: `tuner/src/preset.rs` is `deny_unknown_fields`, so a preset with segments cannot be read there until it mirrors them.

159. **The tuner mirrors the two new sections' *meaning*, not only their spelling, because the schema's safety is computed from the filter that gets built.** `tuner/src/preset.rs` gains `voicing.bridge`, `notes.duplex` and `notes.pan_spread` with the engine's bounds restated (items 148, 153) — the ordinary half of the mirror, checked as always by reading `presets/default.toml` and writing it back byte for byte. The unordinary half is `tuner/src/response.rs`, a copy of `engine::resonance`'s `Biquad`, `fit_backbone` and `max_magnitude` and of `engine::duplex::magnitude`. It exists because `voicing.bridge.backbone` is a *curve* and the engine realises it as a fitted shelf cascade (item 150), so `max|B|` — the quantity both loop bounds are computed against — is a property of the fit and not of the numbers in the file. A tuner that guessed at it would write presets the engine refuses, or refuse presets the engine would have played. The copy is the engine's arithmetic term for term, in the same order and the same precision, and the cross-crate test asserts the two answers are **equal**, not close: `max_magnitude()` and `magnitude()` at seven frequencies, and `duplex::magnitude` at four, all `assert_eq!`. `both_crates_refuse_the_same_broken_voicing` grew from 37 breakages to 70, of which the two that matter are shape-legal and loop-illegal: eight +20 dB peaks stacked on one frequency (which the file never says is +160 dB), and six segments tuned alike on all 88 keys. The engine's own list grew 83 → 88 for the per-key spread.

160. **`notes.pan_spread` was built in the engine because the schema contract needed it and neither engine change had it, and it is four lines.** The contract this workflow was given lists three new sections; `[voicing.bridge]` and `notes.duplex` arrived with items 148–158 and the per-key stereo spread did not, so a tuner-side fit for it had nowhere to write. It is `Option`-shaped in the usual way — an empty table means `voicing.polarization_pan_spread` applies to the whole compass, which is every preset written so far — and the only code that reads it is `Voice::new`, which asks `Preset::pan_spread(key)` instead of the global field. Validated exactly as the scalar is (0..`MAX_PAN_SPREAD`, all 88 or none, each one a pan position on the audio thread). `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk and both reference renders are bit-identical: `render demo` md5 `94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2` and the `render` sweep `ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33`, the hashes item 152 recorded.

161. **The duplex estimator tracks free, and the guard that separates a segment from a partial is 25 cents rather than half a semitone — because a wide guard throws away the very thing being looked for.** `PHYSICS.md` §3 asks for "the existing tracker with the inharmonic seed removed", which the seeded tracker cannot be: it is told where partial `k` is and associates to the nearest peak inside a window no other partial can reach, and the whole claim about a duplex is that it is *not* at `k f0 √(1+Bk²)`. `estimate::duplex::residual_modes` therefore peak-picks every frame, chains peaks by proximity alone, and fits a line to each chain's log amplitude from its own peak onward. The first version guarded ±45 cents around every partial of the note and produced a table whose segments were 60–2000 cents off the grid — i.e. the room and the microphone. The guard is what was wrong: Öberg & Askenfelt's rear-duplex tuning is sharp of nominal by an average approaching **+50 cents**, so a half-semitone guard excludes the median segment on the instrument they measured. What separates a segment from a partial is not frequency but **decay** — these are *release* recordings, the speaking length is being damped in them — so the guard came down to 25 cents (two main lobes of the analysis window at 2.6 kHz, which is where a strong partial's sidelobes throw spurious chains) and `min_t60_s = 0.3 s` does the work `PHYSICS.md` §3 said it would. Three more cuts, each a fact about the instrument rather than a tuning knob: a segment must sit above 1 kHz and above 3 f0 (below that a release recording holds other *speaking lengths* ringing sympathetically — `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5's own centroids for `harmLC3` and `harmLC5` are 314 and 507 Hz — and writing those as segments would model the same energy twice); its loudest frame must be within 0.35 s of the release, because a segment starts ringing when the damper lands and a chain that peaks later is a beat or a broken chain; and its decay must be a straight line in log amplitude to 6 dB RMS, which is what tells one resonator from the sidelobe of a partial. On Salamander: **23 of the 30 sampled keys** got a table, **100 segments** in all, median **+27 cents** off the nearest partial with 50 of the 100 sharp of it. The other 65 keys get an empty row. `PHYSICS.md` §3's instruction — "store measured frequencies, not ratios" — is the one thing the gate proves comes back exactly (item 162).

162. **`DUPLEX_LEVEL_OFFSET_DB` is +93.7 dB, it is constant to a thousandth of a dB over 18 dB of gain, and its *size* is the finding: a normalised segment is culled before it can build.** The gate renders C5 with two segments at the fifth and ninth partials moved +52 and −38 cents — where a real duplex sits — isolates the halo by rendering the same gesture twice, once through the whole instrument and once with nothing to couple through, and subtracts. What comes back exactly is the **frequency**: −0.36 cents, and 52 cents clear of the partial it was displaced from. What comes back linearly is the **level**: over `gain_db` = −26, −20, −14, −8 the render's level moves one dB for one dB, and `gain_db − level` is 93.7116, 93.7116, 93.7118, 93.7118 dB. That is not a gain-staging constant. `gain_db` is normalised to the segment's *steady* response at its own frequency (item 154), so the per-sample input gain the engine builds is `2 G (1 − r)` — about one part in ten thousand at a 1.4 s decay — and the mode must be driven up over its own time constant to reach `G`. `ModalBank`'s culling zeroes any state below `CULL_AMPLITUDE` on every block, and a segment starts below it, so the mode is zeroed before the drive can raise it: what a render shows is its *impulse* response, and a segment asked to ring 1.4 s rings **0.21 s** whatever `t60_s` says. That is the 64 dB between the two, and it means a duplex written from a measurement is inaudible in the engine as it stands. The gate pins all three facts — including the truncated decay — so the day the drive path or the culling changes, the test fails and the constant has to be re-measured rather than quietly becoming wrong. The fix is not on the tuner's side of the file: either the cull has to know that an undamped bank is allowed to be quiet, or `gain_db` has to stop being normalised to a steady response the drive can never establish.

163. **The duplex level is set by what it costs the sympathetic loop, not by the schema's gain ceiling — measured, because the first version cost 5 dB of halo for nothing.** With the offset of item 162, the loudest segment on the Salamander recordings asks for **+41 dB** against the schema's +6, so every row has to be shifted; clamping each row on its own would flatten the compass to one level and throw away the relative structure, which is the part the gate proves is recoverable, so one shift is applied to all of them. Choosing that shift to just fit the +6 dB ceiling (−35 dB) was tried first and is wrong: 88 permanently undamped banks then take **4.16** of the sympathetic loop (item 156's `Σ|D| + max|D|`), which drops the coupling ceiling from 0.05 to 0.026 and then, once the fit lifts the backbone, to 0.0028 — and `harmLC3` comes out at **−55.6 dB against −50.9** with the segments quiet. The segments are inaudible either way (item 162); at the loud setting they are inaudible *and* they spend the halo's whole headroom. So the shift is chosen to leave them a twentieth of the loop (−73.4 dB, measured factor 0.050), the relative structure is preserved, and the coupling gets its ceiling back.

164. **The halo fit closes on the release-resonance targets and cannot reach the between-partial ones at all, and the reason is not the fit.** `estimate::halo` puts `TUNING_REPORT.md` §4 and §5 in one loop: render the engine, measure it with the same code the recordings are measured with, step, render again. Two numbers and a shape are free — `voicing.resonance_coupling`, the backbone's overall gain, and its tilt above Ege & Boutillon's 1.1 kHz transition — and the rest of the backbone is `PHYSICS.md` §4's published mobility read off as data (`MOBILITY_SHAPE`), with the peaks taken from the preset's own `soundboard.body_modes` at §4's measured ±12 dB of fluctuation. There is deliberately **no per-register coupling field**: a register's share of the halo *is* the backbone's shape, because the drive a string receives is `coupling · B(f)` at the frequency it answers on, and a second control for it would be a parameter the model already has. Eight passes, best cost kept, from `presets/salamander-c5.toml`:

| target (`TUNING_REPORT.md`) | want | before | after |
|:--|---:|---:|---:|
| `harmLC3` peak re strike | −31 ± 3 | −70.2 | **−50.9** |
| `harmLC5` peak re strike | −39 ± 3 | −93.3 | **−72.8** |
| between-partial C4 @ 1 s | −45.5 ± 1.5 | −47.1 | −47.1 |
| between-partial C6 @ 1 s | −24 ± 2 | −47.6 | −47.6 |
| between-partial C7 @ 1 s | −9.8 ± 6.25 | −48.1 | −48.1 |

    The release-resonance half moves 19–21 dB in the right direction and stops
    against the loop bound: the fit lands on `resonance_coupling` 0.011554 with
    the backbone at its +20 dB clamp and a tilt of +7.5 dB, a bridge loop gain
    of **0.2500** — exactly item 149's bound — and a duplex loop gain of 0.0125.
    It is still 20 and 34 dB short. The between-partial half does not move *at
    all*: 20× the effective coupling changes C6 and C7 by 0.0 dB and C4 by 0.0
    dB. The mid-range constraint §4 insisted on ("the engine is right here, do
    not wreck it") is therefore held exactly, which is the one target-shaped
    thing this fit achieved by not moving. The reason the treble cannot be
    reached is measurable and is the same mechanism as item 162: with one note
    struck and the pedal up, everything the halo could come from is either
    damped or culled, and the coupling can only raise a signal that something
    is already producing. The whole demo renders 0.5 dB louder at the peak and
    **0.03 dB** louder in RMS after the change, which is the same statement
    from the other end. `TUNING_REPORT.md` §4's C7 figure needs a broadband
    bridge force — the strings' own output is a line spectrum plus an onset
    transient — and that is a change in `string.rs`, not a coupling parameter.

165. **The per-key stereo spread is inverted on a line measured per key, and it is aimed at the band the report is willing to claim rather than at each recording's own number.** `DRIFT_PER_SPREAD_DB` (item 137) is one slope for the whole instrument, and the instrument does not have one: at the global ceiling of 0.4 the engine's own renders drift 0.73 dB at A0 and 8.67 dB at C5. `estimate::directivity::KeyDriftLine` is that key's two ends, both measured on the engine, and `pan_spread_table` inverts each key on its own line and fills the compass with the same monotone-cubic interpolation every other per-note table uses. The target is the recording's drift **clamped into `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5's measured 1.2–6.2 dB band** — a recording that drifts 10.7 dB at C6 is eight partials at the end of a treble decay with three of them in the floor, and asking the engine to reproduce it is asking it to reproduce the floor. Over the 28 sampled keys the engine's rendered drift goes from **15 of 28 inside the band to 22 of 28**, and every overshoot is gone: C5 8.67 → 5.38 dB, C6 7.94 → 6.05, C8 9.97 → 5.73. The keys still outside are all *under* it, in the bottom octave and a half, where the key's pan is already so far left that the spread has nowhere to move it — A0's whole line is 0.03 to 0.73 dB, so no setting of this parameter can reach 1.2. `KeyDriftLine::spread_for` says so rather than dividing by a slope of nearly zero. The gate recovers a known per-key table to within 0.03 where the compass-wide inversion is out by up to 0.08.

166. **What the stage-2 fit is, and what it cost.** `tuner/examples/fit_sympathetic.rs` is the render-and-measure stage `TUNING.md` promised: it reads a library and a stage-1 preset, writes `notes.duplex` from the release recordings, fits the coupling and the admittance against §4 and §5 by rendering the engine, fits `notes.pan_spread` against the recordings' own drift, and emits the preset. Roughly four minutes on an M4 Pro against the warm cache, most of it renders. `presets/salamander-c5.toml` grows by 490 lines: an eleven-anchor backbone, 24 bridge peaks from the board's own modes, 100 duplex segments over 23 keys and an 88-entry spread table. `presets/default.toml` is untouched, and the two reference renders are bit-identical to the hashes of item 152. Four self-calibration tests were added to `tuner/tests/calibration.rs` — `a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it`, `the_halo_level_follows_the_coupling_the_fit_inverts_it_on` (doubling the coupling moves the halo +6.3 dB), `a_known_bridge_tilt_is_the_tilt_the_engines_own_filter_realises` (a 9 dB tilt at 16 kHz realises 6.45 dB at 8 kHz against the 6.67 the anchors ask for, and the mirror equals the engine bit for bit), and `a_known_per_key_spread_comes_back_key_by_key` — plus 21 unit tests across `response.rs`, `estimate/duplex.rs`, `estimate/halo.rs` and `estimate/directivity.rs`. `cargo test --release --workspace`: 358 passed, 0 failed. Clippy clean apart from the `partial_count` dead-code warning that is present on the baseline commit too.

167. **End-to-end validation of the sympathetic milestone: the headline metric did not move, at any key, at any velocity, by more than a tenth of a decibel.** `TUNING_REPORT.md` §4's between-partial energy one second after the strike is what backlog item 5 exists for — the recordings give −44.3 dB at C4 fortissimo, −22.1/−26.4 at C6 and −15.9/−13.0/−3.5 at C7, against the engine's −47.0/−47.6/−48.2 — and the whole milestone (a fitted bridge admittance, 100 duplex segments, a re-fitted coupling) leaves it exactly where it was. Re-measured with `residual.rs`'s own code on twelve renders per preset, `presets/salamander-c5.toml` as it stood at `2daa84d` against the file this milestone wrote, at C4/C6/C7 and velocities 40/68/90/108: **C4 −46.9 → −47.0, −47.0 → −47.0, −47.0 → −47.0, −46.9 → −46.9; C6 −47.3 → −47.3, −47.5 → −47.5, −47.6 → −47.6, −47.6 → −47.6; C7 −46.8 → −46.8, −47.8 → −47.8, −48.1 → −48.1, −48.0 → −48.0.** The recordings measured beside them, through the same code, come back at −40.2/−44.0/−44.1/−44.3 (C4), −22.1/−27.7/−23.3/−26.4 (C6) and −15.9/−13.0/−8.4/−3.5 (C7), which reproduces §4's published table. The gap is therefore not a measurement artefact of the report and it is not closed: it stands at 3 dB at C4, 21 dB at C6 and 44 dB at C7 fortissimo, exactly where §4 left it. `tuner/examples/verify_milestone_b.rs` section 1.

168. **The reason it did not move is that the statistic is sitting on its own analysis floor, which is worth more than the milestone's own result.** Taking the engine apart one path at a time and re-measuring C4/C6/C7 at velocity 108 (section 7 of the same example): as shipped, **−47.2 / −47.6 / −48.0**; with the sympathetic coupling and the duplex removed *entirely*, **−47.2 / −47.6 / −48.0**; with the whole board path removed (`board_mix = 0`), −47.4 / −47.7 / −46.9; with the body modes removed, unchanged; with the diffuse field cut from seconds to 50 ms, unchanged. Nothing in the instrument moves it. What does move it is the analysis window: the same renders read at 43, 85, 341 and 1365 ms give −49.7 / −47.2 / −46.3 / −41.6 at C4 and −45.9 / −48.0 / −28.2 / −10.5 at C7. That is the signature of a leakage floor — a decaying partial is a Lorentzian whose skirts fall outside the guard band, and the longer the window relative to the decay the more of the partial is counted as lying between the partials. So `between@1s` at 85 ms is a *lower bound of about −48 dB* on this instrument, the engine is under it everywhere, and the recordings are 3 to 44 dB above it, which is why the comparison remains valid in the direction the report used it. The consequence for the next attempt is concrete: no change to the halo can be *seen* in this number until it raises the engine's between-partial content above −48 dB, and a fit that servos on it (item 164's `between C6` / `between C7` targets) is servoing on a constant. Twenty times the shipped coupling moves C7 by **+4.1 dB** and C4 and C6 by nothing at all, which is the only evidence in the whole census that the bus reaches this statistic at all.

169. **What the milestone did move, measured on the file it wrote: the release-resonance halo, by 36 and 65 dB, and it no longer dies in a second.** `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5's `harm*` rows are the other half of item 5's evidence, and they are the half that responded. Re-measured on both presets with `fit_sympathetic.rs`'s own gesture (strike, release, subtract the uncoupled render, quote against a strike of the same key, mechanism silenced), `harmLC3` goes **−87.1 → −50.9 dB** against a target of −31 ± 3, and `harmLC5` **−137.4 → −72.8** against −39 ± 3. Both are still short — 20 and 34 dB — but the direction and the size are real and they are the largest single change this milestone made. Isolated by subtraction on the halo phrase itself: a silently held C2/C3/E3/G3 answering a C5-E5-G5 chord peaks at **−85.6 dB below the strike before the milestone and −65.6 dB after it**, +20.0 dB, which is the backbone's fitted gain arriving exactly where the physics says it should; held C3 under G4 (G4 is C3's third partial) goes −75.9 → −62.0. The change in *persistence* is larger than the change in level: a second and a half after the chord the old halo is at −249.5 dB, i.e. culled to nothing, and the new one is at −87.4 dB and still ringing. `verify_milestone_b.rs` section 2.

170. **The duplex table the estimator wrote is inaudible in the engine it was written for — 148 dB below the note — which makes items 162 and 163 a shipped fact rather than a caveat.** Measured on `presets/salamander-c5.toml` by rendering a staccato C6 (one of the 23 keys that got a table, six segments, damper working) twice, once as shipped and once with `notes.duplex` emptied, and subtracting: the segments' entire contribution peaks at **−148.6 dB** relative to the strike and is at −170.3 dB half a second later. The schema-ceiling table of `the_worst_case_with_a_duplex_on_every_key_fits_the_performance_budget` is audible and the mechanism is right; what is not audible is the *measured* table, because item 163 chose its level from the sympathetic loop budget (a twentieth of the loop, −73.4 dB of shift) on top of item 162's +93.7 dB normalisation-versus-culling gap. The honest statement of the feature's status is therefore: the duplex path is built, tested and free, the estimator recovers real segment frequencies to within a cent, and the level at which they ship is a placeholder that no listener can hear. Fixing it is not a tuner change — the cull has to learn that an undamped bank may start quiet, or `gain_db` has to stop being normalised to a steady response that the drive never establishes.

171. **The performance budget survives the measured preset, which is the case that was actually at risk.** `SPEC.md`'s worst case is a sustain-pedal-down glissando that leaves all 88 keys ringing; the duplex made it worse in a way the synthetic test could not show, because the shipped preset also carries a voiced bridge, and a louder halo wakes voices that used to sleep. Best of three 10 s renders on an M4 Pro: **41.6 % of one core** on `presets/salamander-c5.toml` against 39.3 % on the default and 40.9 % with six segments on every key. Measured independently in the example, before against after: 40.3 % → 41.2 %, so the whole milestone costs **0.9 points** on the file that ships it. `the_worst_case_with_the_measured_presets_duplex_fits_the_performance_budget` asserts the 50 % goal on the checked-in preset rather than on a synthetic one, and it is the only performance test with the fitted admittance in it.

172. **Neutrality was proven by subtraction on the file that exercises it, not only on the file that does not.** Item 103's rule is that a preset without the new fields renders what it always rendered, and every previous item verified it on presets that had never had the fields at all. This one takes `presets/salamander-c5.toml` *with* its bridge, its 100 segments and its 88-entry spread table, strips the three sections back out, restores the one scalar the fit moved (`resonance_coupling`), and renders the demo against the preset as it stood at `2daa84d`: **bit-identical, both channels, 720 000 samples.** `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk and its two reference renders are the hashes item 152 recorded — `render demo` md5 `94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2`, `render` sweep `ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33`. The salamander demo is now `2b9f0d3c9a95359f66020ef1a8feb2f5` against item 152's `fac48d3c…`, and that difference is the milestone: the preset on disk changed, which is what it was for.

173. **A legal preset rendered `NaN`, and it took an end-to-end sweep to find it.** `soundboard.rs`'s `line_loss` designs the diffuse field's per-line damping by solving `|(1-a)/(1-a e^-jw)| = rho` for the pole, where `rho` is how much more the line must lose at `fdn_hf_hz` than at DC. When `soundboard.fdn_t60_hf == soundboard.fdn_t60_lf` — a flat diffuse field, which is a perfectly ordinary thing to ask for — `rho` is exactly 1, the denominator `1 - rho^2` is zero, and the closed form is `0/0`: **287 702 of 288 000 samples of the render were not finite**, and `Preset::validate` had no objection, because both numbers are positive and either one on its own is fine. It was found by a validation pass that varied the reverberation time to see what set the between-partial floor (item 168), not by a unit test, because until then nothing had ever asked for a flat field. Fixed where the arithmetic is, not in the validator: `rho` is clamped at unity (a board whose treble outlives its bass is not a board, and this one-pole form cannot make a gain that rises with frequency), and `rho = 1` returns the pole that realises a flat gain, `a = 0`, which is the limit the closed form approaches. `a_flat_diffuse_field_is_a_flat_gain_rather_than_a_division_by_zero` renders 400 blocks of the board at four settings including two equal pairs. Both reference renders are bit-identical across the fix, since every preset in the repository has `fdn_t60_hf < fdn_t60_lf` and the changed branch is not taken.

174. **The instrument now has a phrase about the halo, and it is in the engine rather than in the renderer.** `render::halo_sequence` (30 s, `piano-emulator render out.wav halo`) is three movements, one per path the milestone opened: staccato in the treble with no pedal and nothing held, where what rings between the notes is the undamped top of the instrument and the segments; a silently held C2/C3/E3/G3 struck into from two octaves above and then released while the bloom is still sounding; and a pedal-down wash rolled across five octaves. The chords are chosen so that **every struck partial coincides with a partial of a held string** — C5 is C3's fourth and C2's eighth, E5 is E3's fourth, G5 is G3's fourth — because a string driven off its own partials answers at the driving frequency and at the amplitude its off-resonance mobility allows, which is nothing. The first version of the phrase held A1/E2/A2 under a C major triad, which shares no frequencies with it, and measured the halo at −238 dB: the phrase, not the engine, was what had no halo in it. It lives in `render.rs` beside `demo_sequence` so that the file `renders/salamander-ab/halo_demo.wav` ships and the phrase the REPL plays are the same events, and so the health scan of item 175 runs on exactly the audio a listener is given.

175. **The renders are clean, and clean in the same places they were before.** Non-finite samples, clipping, DC and derivative outliers over the demo, the pedal phrase, the halo phrase and 45 s of dense random playing with the pedal working, on `presets/salamander-c5.toml` and on the preset as it stood before the milestone: **no non-finite samples anywhere, nothing at or above 0 dBFS, peaks −4.3 to −9.7 dBFS, DC between −117 and −203 dB.** The derivative scan finds the same handful of outliers on both presets at the same times with the same ratios — 14.4 → 14.8 at 3.91 s of the demo (a pedal release), 14.3 at 13.03 s, 12.6 in the dense material — so the milestone introduced no discontinuity; the largest ratio in the halo phrase itself is 10.6, under the scan's threshold. `verify_milestone_b.rs` section 3, which prints both presets so the comparison is on the page rather than in a memory of a previous run.

176. **Running the acceptance suite against the measured preset found two test bugs and one real limit, and the two bugs were both `MAX_COUPLING` outliving its meaning.** `PIANO_PRESET=presets/salamander-c5.toml cargo test --test acceptance` is the mode item 108's header describes — the way a model-level assertion gets told apart from a window around one particular piano. It had never been run on a preset with a bridge in it. Five tests failed; three of the failures were the tests' own fault. (a) `a_prepared_string_rings_on_after_the_note_that_excited_it` and `a_duplex_segment_answers_another_keys_note_through_the_bridge` both set `resonance_coupling = resonance::MAX_COUPLING` and then `expect` the preset to be legal, which stopped being true the day item 149 made the bound a property of the *loop*: on the measured preset that is a loop gain of **1.08** and the validator refuses it, so both tests panicked before measuring anything. They now ask `strongest_legal_coupling`, which walks the scalar down until `Preset::validate` accepts rather than keeping a second copy of the bound in the test file — and the duplex test now clears the base preset's own segments on both sides, since a control that carries a hundred of them differs from the instrument under test by more than the one segment it adds. (b) `the_most_extreme_bridge_a_preset_may_ask_for_stays_bounded_for_a_minute` spaced its 2400 notes 25 ms apart, which puts the last note at exactly 60.0 s and leaves its "after the playing stopped" window still being played into: it passed on the default preset by 0.06 dB and failed on the measured one by 0.08, and neither number was about stability. The notes are now 20 ms apart (50 a second, denser, over the first 48 s), the comparison has seven seconds of real decay in it, and the assertion asks for **10 dB** of fall rather than any fall at all. Measured over 90 s on both presets, the instrument at the loop ceiling decays 17 → 35 → 49 → 60 dB down over the thirty seconds after the last note: the contract holds with room to spare, and the test now says so instead of nearly saying the opposite.

177. **The real limit the sweep found: on the measured preset a sympathetically excited string is culled the moment its exciter stops.** With the mechanism silenced and the coupling at the strongest the preset may legally ask for — the bound is **0.0116** (the fitted bridge peaks at 26.7 dB, i.e. 21.6x, and 0.25/21.6 is what is left for the scalar), and the helper's quarter-steps land on 0.0089, against the default preset's **0.05** — a silently held C3 under a fortissimo G4 reaches −84 dBFS while the note sounds and **−174 dBFS a second and a half after it stops**. It is not decaying, it is being zeroed: `types::IDLE_ENERGY` is −100 dBFS and the string never gets far enough above it to have anything to ring on with. On the default preset the same test passes, because the flat bus lets the coupling be four times higher. That is the same finding as items 168 and 169 from the other end — the halo rose 36 dB and is still 20 dB short of the recordings — with a sharper statement of the mechanism: *a voiced bridge spends the coupling budget on its peaks, and the frequencies that are not on a peak end up less coupled than they were before the bridge existed.* Two of the five failures under `PIANO_PRESET` are the documented window kind (C7's fundamental T60 comes out 3.99 s against the suite's 0.3–2 s band, and A2's eighth partial is stretched 4.5 cents against the 6 the suite asks for) and are reported measured-versus-window, not fixed.

178. **Independent audio audit of the sympathetic milestone (`tuner/examples/independent_audit.rs`), with its own DSP, and what it confirms, sharpens and contradicts.** Every spectral measurement in the audit — the FFT, the exact-frequency Hann projection, the T60 fits, the between-partial band split — is implemented in the example itself rather than imported from `residual.rs` or `estimate::halo`, so it cross-checks the tuner's measurement code as well as the engine. Eight criteria, re-measured from rendered audio and the recordings side by side. **(1) Census, confirmed unmoved and the floor explanation confirmed from a new angle:** at the report's 85 ms window C7 fortissimo reads recording −3.4 dB against the engine's −48.0 before *and* after (C6 −26.4 vs −47.6, C4 −44.3 vs −47.2 both), reproducing items 167–168 to a tenth of a dB with none of their code; at a 341 ms window, where item 168 says the leakage floor lifts, the recording reads −17.0 and the engine −38.5, so **21.5 dB of the C7 gap is real engine deficit measurable above the floor**, not analysis artefact. C4 stays inside −44..−47 everywhere: the mid-range constraint held. **(2) Release halo, short and — new finding — it does not ring:** the fit's own gesture re-implemented gives `harmLC3` −59.6 dB peak against the target −31 ± 3 and `harmLC5` −86.0 against −39 ± 3 (the fit's report said −50.9/−72.8 on its slightly different windowing; both statements are 20–47 dB short), and the halo's envelope falls 20 dB within **0.15 s** of the release and is at −161 dB half a second after it — the recordings ring 1–2 s, so the *persistence* half of §5's target fails harder than the level half. Item 169's persistence claim was measured on a chord over silently held keys and does not transfer to the single strike-release the `harm*` samples actually are, because after the release nothing undamped remains (item 177's cull from the other end). **(3) Duplex, dead, including at the ceiling:** on a staccato C6 the shipped table's entire isolated contribution (render minus table-emptied render) peaks at −83.7 dB *during the note* and is at −226..−246 dB at the segment frequencies 0.4 s after the release; at the largest uniform boost the validator accepts (+26 dB) it is −57.7 during and −198..−214 after. No T60 is fittable because there is nothing to fit. The criterion's A/B (peaks present shipped, absent emptied) fails in the only honest direction: both are silence. **(4) Cross-note bloom, mechanism real, level inaudible:** a silently pressed C3 under a staccato G4 answers at C3's third partial 70 dB above the no-press control 0.5 s after the damp (−77.2 vs −146.9 dB re strike) and 206 dB above at 1.5 s — the Cartling path through the bridge works and is frequency-selective — but the same partial is culled to −239 dB by 2.5 s, and against a *sounding* held C3 the struck G4 changes the coincident partial by **−1.2 dB** and every other partial by under 0.05, i.e. the bloom a listener could hear on a held note is below interference. **(5) Decay-rate coupling, absent as item 151 pinned:** the three note-partials nearest the fitted bridge peaks (C1 k3 2.7 c off the 97 Hz peak, and two others) come back with T60 14.45 s shipped against 14.60 unity-bridge and 14.60 uncoupled — a 1 % loop-feedback shortening, not `Re Y`. **(6) Mid-range guard, clean:** A2/C4 worst partial move 0.00 cents, T60s within 0.06 s, RMS over 3 s within 0.05 dB, peak within 0.07 dB, before against after. **(7) Stability, holds:** 60 s of dense pedal-down playing (a note every 40–140 ms, pedal never lifted) at the shipped coupling, at 0.999× the validator's bound (0.011542 — the shipped 0.011554 *is* the bound, which restates item 164's "fit stopped against the loop bound"), and on an all-anchors-+20 dB bridge at its own bound (0.004828): all finite, peaks −7.6 dBFS, block-RMS trend +0.03 dB/s over the last 20 s of still-dense playing — no growth. **(8) Stereo drift, in band where it can be:** A0 0.38 dB (under, structurally — item 165), A2 1.30, C4 2.48, C5 4.84, C7 5.07 inside the recordings' 1.2–6.2 dB, C6 6.79 marginally over on this audit's 171 ms windows (the fit's own measure reads 6.05 there; the key has four usable partials, so ±0.7 dB is window choice, not disagreement). Neutrality re-verified from the binary: the default demo and sweep render to md5 `94d9d076…`/`ad120191…`, item 172's exact hashes, and `presets/default.toml` is untouched. Net verdict: the milestone's engineering contracts (stability, neutrality, cost, mid-range) all hold under independent measurement; of the sound targets, only the stereo drift is met — the treble aftersound, the release halo's level *and* duration, the duplex audibility and the decay-rate coupling all fail, and every failure traces to the same two mechanisms the implementation itself documented: the cull zeroing quiet-but-rising resonators (162/170/177) and the absence of a broadband bridge drive plus `Re Y` damping (164/151).

179. **The loop-gain measurement was evadable by a preset the schema allows, and the hole was worth 15.6 dB — a certified loop gain of 0.25 realising 1.5.** Item 149 made the stability bound a property of the *realised* filter, measured on a 512-point log grid plus every peak's own centre frequency. That is not enough, and the reason is that the filter is a **cascade**: the sections' decibels *add*, so the maximum of two overlapping resonances lies strictly *between* their centres, where neither the grid nor the centres look. Reproduced on the checked-in code: twenty `Q`-50 +20 dB peaks at 101.63 Hz and twenty at 102.32 Hz — both centres inside one grid interval (101.28–102.66 Hz), every field inside the schema — measured **654.6 dB** and reached **670.1 dB** at 101.83 Hz on a four-million-point scan. A preset fitted to the measured figure (`coupling = 0.25/measured`, legal) therefore realised a loop gain of **1.494**: past self-sustainment, with `DRIVE_CEILING` holding the samples finite while the loop rode the clamp as a tone that never decays — the exact failure the 60 s fuzz test's 10 dB decay assertion defines as broken. Milder splits ate most of the margin rather than all of it (101.63/102.0: 23.0 dB hidden). The fix is to scan what actually has curvature: the coarse grid still covers the smooth half (shelf transitions and peak skirts, where a bell changes by ≤0.12 dB per 1.4 % step and monotonically, so no maximum can hide there), and every resonance now gets a fine linear scan ±8 bandwidths at 64 samples per bandwidth, each window's best sample refined by golden section over one sampling step. A maximum can only hide between two samples if the curve turns inside the interval, which needs curvature at that scale, which needs a peaking section within a few bandwidths — and that section's own window covers it. Measured against dense scans the residue is **0.000 dB** on all five adversarial splits and 0.0000 dB on the shipped preset, and the same construction now certifies a realised loop gain of 0.2500. Cost: 2.8 ms for the shipped bridge, 3.4 ms at the schema's ceiling (40 peaks), at preset-load time only — `Biquad::magnitude` now takes the four trigonometric terms precomputed per frequency, which is what keeps 42 k evaluations through 47 sections at that price. `tuner/src/response.rs` mirrors it term for term, double-angle identity included, because `calibration.rs` asserts the two crates' answers are equal **bit for bit** and a bound that differed in the last bit would let a preset through one crate and not the other. Two tests pin it: `the_measured_maximum_cannot_be_hidden_between_grid_points` (five splits against a two-million-point dense scan) and `an_accepted_preset_realises_the_loop_gain_it_was_accepted_for`, which is the property rather than the proxy.

180. **`ResonanceBus::set_coupling` promised something `Preset::validate` had never proved, and now the bus carries its own bound.** The method clamped to `MAX_COUPLING` and said it was safe because "`Preset::validate` bounded `MAX_COUPLING * max|B|`'s worst case at load time". It did not: `validate_bridge` bounds `resonance_coupling · max|B|` — the value in the *file* — which says nothing about what a later caller may set. Measured: `ResonanceBus::from_preset(presets/salamander-c5.toml)` followed by `set_coupling(MAX_COUPLING)` gives coupling 0.05 against a bridge peaking at 26.7 dB, i.e. a realised loop gain of **1.082** — the very number item 176 cites as the reason the validator refuses that preset. No caller does it today (the "live control from the REPL" the comment referenced does not exist; `grep` finds no call outside the module and its tests), but it is a `pub` method on a `pub` type and its documented invariant was false. `Preset::max_safe_coupling` now computes the whole contract as one number — the smallest of `MAX_COUPLING`, `MAX_BRIDGE_LOOP_GAIN / max|B|` and, when there are segments, `MAX_DUPLEX_LOOP_GAIN / (max|B| · duplex response)` — `ResonanceBus` stores it at construction from the filter it *built*, and `set_coupling` clamps against that. A flat bus's ceiling is exactly `MAX_COUPLING`, so nothing about a preset without a bridge changes, which `set_coupling_cannot_walk_a_bridged_bus_past_its_loop_bound` asserts from both ends: the bound is enforced and it is not gratuitously tighter than the bound.

181. **The shipped coupling was fitted onto the exact edge of legality — 0.000 dB of headroom — and a fit must aim at the bound minus a margin, not at the bound.** Measured on the file as it stood: `max|B| = 21.6381` (26.70 dB), `resonance_coupling = 0.011553708`, loop gain **0.250000** against a strictly-greater refusal test, with the realised maximum already 0.0002 dB above the sampled one. Item 164's "the fit stopped against the loop bound" is that statement from the other end. It is not survivable: item 179's sharper measurement moved `max|B|` by 0.0002 dB and the engine promptly **refused its own shipped preset**. `estimate::halo::LOOP_GAIN_FIT_MARGIN` is now 0.9 and `HaloVoicing::coupling_ceiling` targets `0.9 · MAX_BRIDGE_LOOP_GAIN`; the shipped scalar is the same fit stopping at the new ceiling, `0.9 · 0.25 / 21.638557 = 0.010398103`, and `the_shipped_presets_have_real_headroom_against_the_loop_bound` refuses any shipped file above 95 % of its own bound. The cost is 0.9 dB of halo. **Refuted, in passing:** the related claim (item 177, restated by the review) that a voiced bridge leaves off-peak frequencies *less* coupled than the flat bus did. It does not, because the fit spent its freedom on the backbone: the shipped backbone stands at +17.6 to +20 dB across the whole midrange, so the *effective* coupling `coupling · |B(f)|`, at the scalar as it was fitted, is 0.060 at 50 Hz, 0.165 at 130 Hz, 0.079 at 262 Hz, 0.119 at 1 kHz and 0.116 at 4 kHz — and 0.9 dB under each of those after this item's margin — against the flat bus's 0.05 everywhere. The scalar fell from 0.05 to 0.0116 and the coupling *rose*; what the peaks cost is only their 6.0 dB of crest over the backbone, since the bound applies to the maximum.

182. **`Re Y` landed where `PHYSICS.md` §4 says it belongs — the string's own per-partial damping — and a partial on a board mode now decays 22 % faster instead of 1 %.** Item 151 measured that the bus cannot produce the decay-rate half of §4 and pinned its absence; the review confirmed it independently on the shipped preset (T60 14.45 s against 14.60 s unity-bridge, a loop-feedback shortening). The new field is `[voicing.bridge].radiated_share`: the share of each partial's decay rate that is loss into the board, so `sigma_k <- sigma_k · (1 + share · (|P(f_k)| − 1))` with `P` the **peaks alone**. The backbone is deliberately not in it — `notes.sigma0` and `notes.sigma1` are fitted to recorded decays, so the mean mobility is already inside them once and a term proportional to `|B|` would count it twice and retune the whole compass. What the smooth fitted law cannot carry is the mode-to-mode fluctuation, which is exactly the peaks. The share is a physical quantity rather than a knob: Woodhouse's body-loss/air-loss ratio for the piano exceeds 0 dB above ~160 Hz, so more than half of what a partial loses up there goes into the board, and `estimate::halo::RADIATED_SHARE` ships the conservative end of that statement, 0.5, unfitted — like `MOBILITY_SHAPE`, read off the literature §4 already cites. The factor is clamped to a factor of four either way (`string::RADIATED_FACTOR_RANGE`), both ends far outside the measured ±10–15 dB of board fluctuation, so it bounds pathological presets and nothing else; `radiated_share` itself is bounded at 0.9 so a deep enough anti-resonance cannot cancel a partial's damping. Absent or zero the factor is exactly 1.0 and every string is built bit for bit as before. Measured on the render, not on the coefficient (`string::tests::a_partial_on_a_board_mode_decays_faster_than_the_fitted_law`): a +6 dB board mode on the fundamental of C6 shortens the vertical bank's decay to within 5 % of the predicted 1.50×, and a zero share moves it by nothing (the rendered rate is the designed one to within 5 %, i.e. the factor really is exactly 1). End to end on the shipped preset, the review's own criterion 5, re-run: C1's third partial 2.7 cents off the 97 Hz board mode comes back at **T60 11.33 s against 14.60 s** with a unity bridge (was 14.45), and 11.42 s with the coupling switched off entirely — i.e. the shortening is the string's own damping and not loop feedback, which is the distinction the signature is about. The two other probes move 16.51→12.86 s and 8.67→7.34 s. `resonance::tests::the_bridge_does_not_move_the_struck_strings_own_decay` is unchanged and still passes: the *bus* still cannot do this, and must not be expected to.

183. **What the `harmL*` halo targets are actually made of, measured — and criterion 2 is not comparing like with like.** `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5 quotes `harmLC3` at −30.7 dB and `harmLC5` at −39.0 dB relative to a strike of the same key and reads them as "a target for the sympathetic-coupling fit"; the fit and the audit both measure the engine against them by rendering the gesture twice and subtracting the uncoupled render, i.e. by measuring the engine's **sympathetic component alone**. Those are the same quantity only if the recording contains nothing else. It does not. New section 9 of `independent_audit.rs` splits both signals into the struck key's own partials and everything else, on the same 341 ms window: **`harmLC3` is 80 % the note's own partials** — a damper takes a few tenths of a second to stop a wound string and the recording contains that — and the engine's *whole* post-release signal, damper working and nothing subtracted, reads **−24.1 dB against the recording's −30.7 with a 76 % own-partial share**. Like for like the engine is 6.6 dB *louder* than the target at C3, not 29 dB quieter; the 29 dB is the artefact of quoting a coupling-only residual against a recording that is mostly damped string. At C5 the same measurement gives engine −54.6 dB against the recording's −34.5 (70 % own partials, 40 % in the engine), so **20 dB of that target is a real deficit** and survives the correction. The *duration* half survives at both keys and gets a new owner: measured identically on recording and render, the recording's release tail falls 20 dB in **0.50 s (C3) and 0.60 s (C5)** and the engine's in **0.15 s and 0.10 s**. That is the damper, not the coupling — the engine's damper is roughly three times too brisk on this preset — and it is a fit (`notes.damper_sigma`, `voicing.damper_weight`) against the release recordings, not a mechanism that is missing. Left undone deliberately: a damper refit changes every note's release in the instrument and is worth doing from the recordings rather than from two keys.

184. **The treble aftersound gap is on the board's diffuse field, not on the sympathetic coupling — 17 to 28 dB of it, measured by taking each path to an extreme.** `TUNING_REPORT.md`'s backlog item 5 says of §4's headline gap that "the level is a coupling parameter (`resonance.rs`, one per-register field)". Section 10 of `independent_audit.rs` tests that claim by moving one path at a time to its limit and re-measuring between-partial energy on the 341 ms window where item 168 showed the leakage floor lifts. At C7 fortissimo, one second in, against the recording's **−17.0 dB**: shipped **−38.5**, with the sympathetic coupling and the duplex removed altogether −38.6, and with the coupling raised to `Preset::max_safe_coupling` — the largest value the stability contract will ever certify — **−38.5**. The coupling moves this statistic by **0.1 dB at most, in either direction**. The board's diffuse field moves it by 17 dB (`fdn_t60` ×4: **−21.5**, within 4.5 dB of the recording) and at C6 by 28 dB (−49.0 → −20.9 against the recording's −20.5). This also settles the arithmetic question the review raises implicitly: the effective coupling is bounded by `MAX_BRIDGE_LOOP_GAIN`, so no legal preset can put more than 0.25 anywhere, the shipped preset already runs 0.06–0.17 across the midrange (item 181), and the whole remaining headroom in the sympathetic path is 4–6 dB against a 21.5 dB gap. The gap cannot be closed there and never could be. What it needs is the late field — `soundboard.fdn_t60_lf` / `fdn_t60_hf`, shipped at 0.40 / 0.10 s — fitted against the recordings, with `PHYSICS.md` §9's question answered first: how much of what those recordings still hold a second after a treble note is the instrument and how much is the room it was recorded in. Deliberately not done here by hand: a diffuse field four times longer is audible on every note in the instrument and is a fit, not a knob to turn in a review.

185. **What this pass changed in the sound, and what it did not.** `presets/default.toml` is byte-identical and the default demo and compass sweep still render to md5 `94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2` and `ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33` — item 172's hashes, unchanged for the third time, which is the neutrality rule stated as a binary artefact. `presets/salamander-c5.toml` changed in exactly two numbers: `resonance_coupling` 0.011553708 → 0.010398103 (item 181, −0.9 dB of halo) and a new `[voicing.bridge].radiated_share = 0.5` (item 182). Measured end to end on the review's own criteria: the release halo at C3 moved −59.6 → −58.6 dB and at C5 −86.0 → −86.8 (i.e. not at all, as items 183 and 184 explain it cannot); the duplex is unchanged and still inaudible; the decay-rate coupling went from a 1 % shortening to 22 %. Under `PIANO_PRESET=presets/salamander-c5.toml` the acceptance suite reads **19 of 22**, the same count as item 177, but not the same three: `a_prepared_string_rings_on_after_the_note_that_excited_it` — item 177's "real limit", a sympathetically excited string culled the moment its exciter stopped — now **passes**, because `radiated_share` lets the partials sitting in the board's troughs ring long enough to stay above the cull. In its place `the_pedal_makes_a_sound_going_down_and_coming_up` now fails, and it is the same pathology as item 181 in a *test*: the assertion is `|up − strike + 42.4| < 4.0`, the preset's own estimated pedal-up level is −45.61 dB, the engine delivers it 0.79 dB low (item 145's documented delivery error), and the ratio was **−46.3962 dB before this pass and −46.4018 after** — passing by four thousandths of a decibel and failing by two, moved by the 0.008 dB that `radiated_share` adds to a C4 strike peak. It is reported measured-against-window rather than fixed, with the other two documented ones (C7's fundamental at 3.99 s against a 0.3–2 s band, A2's eighth partial stretched 4.5 cents against 6): the honest repair is the 0.8 dB delivery error in the mechanism calibration, and that path is shared with the default preset, whose render is contractually bit-identical.
186. **The realism benchmark is the project's standing fitness function** (`tuner/src/{sampler,realism}.rs`, `tuner/examples/realism_bench.rs`, scoreboard in `renders/realism/REALISM.md`). Ground truth is the Salamander recordings themselves played back by our own SFZ sampler (bit-exact against the raw FLACs, every opcode the file uses honoured, interpretations documented and pinned by tests), so any MIDI phrase renders as engine-vs-real-piano pairs from one event list. Metrics: multi-resolution log-mel distance (the stage-2 loss, now one implementation shared with future fitting), modulation-spectrum distance, attack tonality, band-envelope correlation, release-tail delta — each quoted against its measured noise floor (reference vs itself from the neighbouring velocity layer). First reading (phrase set v1, preset salamander-c5): mean mel 5.34 dB vs 1.59 floor; convicted in order — attacks too tonal (+5.2 dB mean), treble modulation missing (22-24 dB in the 9-13 kHz bands), the engine's energy leaving earlier than the piano's (staccato treble envelope r=0.745 vs 0.994 floor), and band-specific level errors (quiet at 93 Hz and 1.7-3.4 kHz, hot at 526 Hz). Verified independently (numpy reimplementation matches to 0.01 dB; sampler error bar three orders below the signal). The scoreboard format is diffable and versioned; future milestones must move its columns.

187. **Three per-partial fields, and the shape of the two ragged ones is the estimator's shape rather than the schema's convenience.** `notes.partial_gains` and `notes.partial_sigma_scale` are `Vec<Vec<f32>>`: absent, or one row per key, with each row free to be **short** — a tracker measures as far up a series as the recording lets it, and `TUNING_REPORT.md` §3's measurement is a per-note, per-partial residual that exists for the partials it could resolve and for no others. Everything past the end of a row is exactly 1.0, so a key with three entries and a key with none are the same instrument from partial four up (`string::PartialShaping`, `a_short_or_missing_per_partial_row_is_the_unshaped_string`). What is refused, with the key named in the message, is a row **longer** than that key's partial count: C8 has two partials, a row of nine is a table measured on a different tuning or a different Nyquist cap, and the entries the engine would silently drop are precisely the evidence of that. The check runs *after* the partial-series check, so the count a row is measured against is a count of a bank the engine will really build; `notes.comb_floor` is checked before it instead, because `string_params` reads it and the series check calls `string_params`. Ranges: gains 0.1..=10 (±20 dB — the recordings' worst partial is 12–29 dB off a smooth envelope, `TUNING_REPORT.md` §3, and the ladder's deepest is 9.3–17.7 dB, §4a), sigma scale 0.25..=4 (a correction to a *fitted* decay, not a second decay law). Both are velocity-independent by construction: they are properties of the excitation and of the string, and a per-velocity table here would be a second hammer model with no measurement behind it.

188. **The comb floor keeps the comb's sign, and a floor of zero is a branch rather than an algebraic identity.** The excitation magnitude is `sqrt(sin^2(k pi x) + floor^2) * taper * gain`, and `sqrt(x^2)` is *not* `|x|` in `f32` — squaring rounds, and the root of the rounded square misses the original by an ulp or two. Since item 103 requires a neutral field to be bit-exact and not merely equal to seven digits, `comb_magnitude` takes the bare `sin` when the floor is zero and the formula otherwise. It also multiplies by `sin`'s **sign**: the comb's sign is the phase partial `k` starts at, `|sin|` would flip half the series, and at an exact node `signum` gives ±1 so the partial gets the floor instead of nothing. Measured on A2, the key `renders/timbre-ladder/ANALYSIS.md` §4a convicts: the comb's null is k = 17 at **−32.8 dB** under the fundamental bare and **−17.1 dB** with a floor of 0.05, against the recording's deepest partial anywhere of −9.3 to −17.7 dB — i.e. one number moves the engine's worst artefact into the band the piano actually occupies. The same floor moves every partial with a comb above 0.3 by less than **0.1 dB** and every partial at the crest by less than 0.02 dB, because the relative lift is `floor^2/2c^2`: it is a floor, not a tilt (`the_comb_floor_lifts_the_null_partials_and_leaves_the_others_alone`).

189. **`partial_sigma_scale` is applied to the note's fitted rate *before* the polarization factor, the per-string factor and the admittance factor — which is what makes the damper follow it.** `sigma_k <- partial_sigma(k) * scale_k * vertical_factor * unison_scale * radiated_k`, and the damper profile becomes `damper_sigma * damper_weight(f_k) * scale_k`. Ordering it first is not cosmetic: it means the field is a correction to the *measurement* `notes.sigma0`/`sigma1` carry, so both banks of all three strings and the felt's grip inherit it, and a partial the preset says is more lossy than the fitted law is more lossy with the damper on it too. Multiplying by a neutral 1.0 is exact in IEEE-754, so inserting the factor left every existing preset's poles bit for bit where they were — verified by render, not by reasoning (item 194). Measured on the render rather than on the coefficient: a fundamental scaled by 0.5 decays at half the designed rate to within 5 %, and the layout follows the row on both polarizations and in the damper profile to 1e-6 (`a_partial_sigma_scale_changes_that_partials_decay_and_its_damper`).

190. **The band limit became a property of the event because the hammer is not part of the action.** `noise.rs`'s 2 kHz ceiling is Askenfelt's dummy-mass measurement of what the *action* puts into the bridge — a key-off, a damper lift, a pedal tray — and it is right for those four, which is why they keep it as a module constant and `EventShape::new` now takes their `BANDWIDTH_HZ` explicitly rather than reaching for it. A hammer meeting a string is not structure-borne: the felt's contact noise and the string's own onset radiate directly, and both measurements that convict the engine here describe something broadband — attacks **+5.2 dB more tonal** than the recordings over six phrases (`renders/realism/REALISM.md`, worst `staccato` +11.3 dB) and a 30 ms residual **11.1–12.7 dB more tonal** at all three ladder keys, whose flatness is closed at every key by mixing the recording's own residual back in (`renders/timbre-ladder/ANALYSIS.md` §8.3). So `[noise.strike]` states its own `bandwidth_hz`, up to 8 kHz. It is not the transient `TUNING_REPORT.md` §4 refuted — that measured broadband *energy* between the partials over 85 ms and found the engine within ~7 dB — but the residual's *spectrum*, which is compatible with that refutation and is the thing a 2 kHz-limited burst cannot supply. Measured: over 3.5–8 kHz a 6 kHz-limited burst holds **10.3×** what the same colour holds through the action's ceiling.

191. **`[noise.strike]`'s default is silence, and it is the only one of the five for which silence is the neutral value.** Item 103's convention is that a field a preset omits describes the model as it was; `[noise]`'s four action events break it deliberately, because `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5 *measured* them and a preset that omits them should get the piano rather than a mute action. Nothing measured a hammer on its own — a `rel*` recording isolates a release and no library isolates a blow — so a level here would be a guess with the authority of a default. `StrikeNoise::default` is therefore −200 dB, which `noise::SILENT_AMPLITUDE` refuses to render at all, so the neutral value is bit-exact silence and not a very quiet thump; the shape fields carry a plausible hammer (1.2 kHz, 6 kHz, 50 ms) so that a preset which writes only `level_db` gets one. The whole section is `skip_serializing_if` while it equals that default, which is what keeps `presets/salamander-c5.toml` — which *has* a `[noise]` table — byte for byte what it was, and keeps the tuner's `deny_unknown_fields` copy able to read it until the mirror is written.

192. **The strike's level is quoted against a velocity-90 strike like every other event, so its nominal drive is velocity 90 and not the key events' 64.** `TUNING_REPORT.md` §5's whole table is "peak re a velocity-90 strike of the same key", and `calibrate.rs` measures both halves of that ratio through the finished chain. The strike joins it as a fifth shape: `MechanismCalibration` gains a fifth `board_gain`, and `strike(key)` is the same interpolated strike peak divided by it. The calibration's own scratch voices are built with `MechanismCalibration::silent`, whose levels are zero, so the reference strike still contains no mechanism of its own and the construction does not need itself. Delivered: the burst alone lands at **−19.76 dB** against a table asking for −20, and through the voice and the board, averaged over sixteen blows, at **−18.3 dB**. The residual is the documented one — the peak of a filtered noise burst is a random variable that scatters 5–7 dB per draw (item 114), the shape's normalisation is estimated from eight of them, and two eight-draw means of *this* shape differ by 2.8 dB — so no single blow lands on the table and none should.

193. **The strike gets a `Burst` of its own, because a staccato is a release eighty milliseconds into an attack.** The action's four events share one burst per voice on the argument that a key can only be in one place at a time, and that argument is true of them and false of the hammer: a note-off 80 ms after a note-on is the commonest gesture in `renders/realism/REALISM.md`'s `staccato` phrase, and one burst would have every release cut the attack noise of the note it belongs to. The two mix into one block at the same pan and reach the board through the same single `add_voice`, so the cost of the pair is one extra filter chain while both are sounding and nothing at all otherwise. Its seed is `seed_of(key, frame ^ STRIKE_SEED_SALT)`: a note-on and a note-off of one key can land on the same block, and without the salt the two bursts would run the same noise at two levels and sum as one louder event — the same failure the key half of `seed_of` exists to prevent between neighbours. A re-strike retriggers it with the ringing-burst semantics that already existed (state kept, envelope restarted), which is what stops a repeated note from clicking.

194. **Neutrality, proven by render.** `presets/default.toml` is unchanged on disk, is still character for character what the engine writes, and its two reference renders are item 172's hashes for the fourth time: `render demo` md5 `94d9d0764c98fbf76a2cdd3bfa37d3a2`, `render` sweep `ad120191fd5eb16f61e1fe74a3203d33`. `engine/tests/preset.rs`'s sounding-path fingerprint — ten strikes across the compass, taken from the engine at `16307c4` — is unchanged at `63686423443ec4d3`. The demo through `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, which exercises the bridge, the 100 duplex segments and the 88-entry spread table, is bit-identical before and after this change (md5 `fb7e77e43a1653d3fd79efe9784b8f0f`; not item 172's `2b9f0d3c…`, because item 185 moved that preset's `resonance_coupling` and added `radiated_share`), and it re-serialises without a `[noise.strike]` section. That is five new fields, four of which touch `PianoString::new`, none of which moved a sample.

195. **Cost: +0.69 points of one core for both noise paths at once, on material built to make them expensive.** `SPEC.md`'s glissando worst case leaves the bursts idle for most of a render, so the new performance test plays the glissando *and then* a note every 10 ms for the remaining eight seconds — a thousand events on top of 88 ringing strings, with the hammer's noise voiced at −12 dB, an 8 kHz band limit and a 0.3 s decay, which is the most expensive strike the schema allows. Best of three 10 s renders on an M4 Pro: **40.1 %** of one core with the action silenced, **40.8 %** with the mechanism and the hammer's noise both voiced, against the 50 % goal (`the_worst_case_with_the_strike_noise_fits_the_performance_budget`). The three existing worst-case tests are unmoved. The per-partial tables cost nothing at all on the audio path: they are read once, in `PianoString::new`.

196. **Left undone, deliberately: the tuner's copy of the schema.** This pass is the engine half of the contract. `tuner/src/preset.rs` is `deny_unknown_fields`, so a preset carrying `notes.partial_gains`, `notes.partial_sigma_scale`, `notes.comb_floor` or `[noise.strike]` cannot be read there until the mirror states the same five fields and the same bounds, and `tuner/tests/calibration.rs`'s `both_crates_refuse_the_same_broken_voicing` cannot grow the corresponding breakages until it does. Nothing is broken meanwhile — every checked-in preset omits all five, `cargo test --release --workspace` is 418 tests green, and the engine's own list grew from 88 breakages to 117 — but the fields are unreachable from a fitted preset until that half lands. Same caveat as items 158 and 148 in their turn.

197. **The tuner's copy of the schema, and item 196's caveat closed.** `tuner/src/preset.rs` now states the same five fields with the same bounds, the same serde shape and the same field order: `notes.comb_floor` (88 entries, `0..=0.5`, checked *before* the partial-series loop because that loop's own `partial_count` is what the ragged tables are measured against), `notes.partial_gains` and `notes.partial_sigma_scale` (88 rows or none, each row free to be short, a row longer than the key's partial count refused with the key named), and `[noise.strike]` with its own `bandwidth_hz`, its own decay range and the rule that a centroid outside its own band is refused. `PartialLayout` grew `partial_count`, mirroring `engine::string::StringParams::partial_count` in `f32` term for term, and the four action events' shape checks moved into a shared `validate_event` so the strike can pass a different decay range through the same code. `both_crates_refuse_the_same_broken_voicing` grew from **70 breakages to 98** — five for the floor, sixteen for the two ragged tables (including C8's two partials against a row of nine, which is the case the engine names the key for), and eight for the strike, of which one is well formed in every field and still refused (a 4 kHz centroid under a 1 kHz band limit). `a_preset_that_uses_every_refinement_round_trips_through_the_engine` now writes all five and reads them back byte for byte.

198. **The floor decides how deep the comb may go; the gains place every partial inside it — and that is the whole of the split, because it is arithmetic rather than a rule about keys.** `notes.comb_floor` is fitted **first**, from the depth of the recording's own deepest partial below a smooth envelope, and `notes.partial_gains` are then measured against the comb *with that floor already in it* (`estimate::shaping::partial_gains` takes an `EngineComb` and the floor is in its denominator). Neither mechanism can carry the other's correction because the floor is inside every gain by construction. Two consequences decide that this is the right way round and not the other: where a key's bare comb is already *shallower* than the recording's deepest partial the fitted floor is exactly zero — a floor can only lift a null and lifting one the measurement did not ask for is an invention, and thirteen of Salamander's thirty sampled keys are in that case, all of them in the bass where `TUNING_REPORT.md` §3 measured the recordings' worst partial at 21–29 dB; and the schema's ±20 dB is the reason the gains cannot be asked to do it, since a bare null 42 dB deep needs +25 dB to fill. Measured on the engine's own render of A1 with a known floor: the worst gain asked for is **4.0 dB against the fitted floor and 7.8 dB against the bare comb** (`a_known_comb_floor_comes_back_off_the_line_the_engine_draws`).

199. **The comb floor is a line inverted on the engine, not a formula — because how deep a partial *measures* is not how deep the comb *is*, and the gap is 6 dB.** The first implementation matched a measured depth to the comb's own minimum and came back at 0.38 for a known 0.12. Two things separate the two numbers and both are large: **leakage** — a partial 20 dB under its neighbours, measured in a window whose Hann sidelobes are 31 dB down, reads several dB high, and on the engine's render of C4, where the comb is exactly `sin(k pi x)` and its null over the measured partials is 17.3 dB deep, the deepest partial measures **11.0 dB** down — and the **smooth reference**, a degree-2 polynomial in `ln k` that absorbs part of a dip as broad as a comb null. Neither is a defect: the recording goes through exactly the same two. So `CombLine` renders the key at four floors spanning the schema's range, measures each render's deepest partial with the code the recording was measured with, and interpolates — piecewise-linearly in `10^(dB/20)`, which is very nearly straight (C4: 0.282, 0.375, 0.485, 0.630 at floors 0, 0.12, 0.24, 0.40) because a floor adds in quadrature under a null. This is `estimate::directivity`'s pattern (items 137–138) on a third parameter. The gate recovers a known 0.12 as **0.095** at A1, inside the 0.05 the test asks for.

200. **`partial_sigma_scale` is inverted from the partial's T60 through the engine's composite envelope, not read off the two-exponential fit's fast component — and the gate is what says so, by a factor of 1.7.** `DecayFit::fast.sigma` is the right quantity for the `sigma(f)` *line*: `survey::prompt_decay_curve` uses it and `TUNING_REPORT.md` §1 says why (the last twenty decibels of a T60 are extrapolation, and the prompt rate is what the ear calls the decay). It is the wrong quantity for a *per-partial* correction. On the engine's own renders of A1 through `presets/default.toml`, where every partial is on its law by construction and the answer has to be 1.0 everywhere, the prompt rate returns **1.73, 1.72, 1.62, 1.40, 1.43, 1.33, 1.12, 1.18** for partials one to eight — the decomposition trading amplitude against rate between its own two components, differently at every partial. The same renders through `sigma_v = t60_constant(split) / T60` return **1.05, 1.05, 1.04, 1.03, 1.04, 1.03, 1.01, 1.02**. The constant is one bisection per preset, not per partial: the composite `(e^{-s t} + g e^{-r s t})/(1+g)` depends on `s` and `t` only through their product, and the split is global in the engine.

201. **A `partial_sigma_scale` row is divided by its own geometric mean, for the reason `voicing.unison_sigma_scale` is required to average to one.** `notes.sigma0` and `notes.sigma1` decide how long the note rings; this field decides how its partials share that. A row with a mean in it would silently retune the note behind the table's back — and it *would* have one, because the note's own `sigma(f)` line was fitted from the prompt rate (item 200) and this measurement is a T60, so the two conventions differ by a per-note factor that is not a per-partial fact about anything. Normalising is also what lets both live in one preset. The mean is taken over the entries that were *measured*: an unmeasured partial is 1.0 because nothing was learned about it, not because it was measured at the row's average. A row with only one trusted partial therefore comes back **empty** — one partial says nothing about a distribution — which `a_partial_the_decay_stage_does_not_trust_is_left_at_one` asserts. The gate recovers a known row at A1 to within 10 % on all eight scaled partials.

202. **`[noise.strike]` is the onset residual, and three properties of that measurement decided the fit more than the recordings did.** The subtraction is the ladder's, promoted out of `timbre_ladder.rs` into `residual::{track_complex, resynth_locked, onset_residual}` so that what the ladder listened to and what the preset is fitted from are one piece of code.
    * **It starts half a window after the strike.** A hopped projection has nothing to say about a signal it has not seen a whole window of: at the strike half the first window is the silence before it, the partials are measured at about half their amplitude, and the resynthesis leaves the other half behind. Measured on the engine's own render of C4, where the model is exactly true and the residual should be nothing, it stands at **−5 dB** under the note from the strike and at **−16 dB** from half a window later. `FitSpan` already applies the same rule one measurement upstream.
    * **The shape is fitted over 200 Hz – 8 kHz, and the level is measured on the same band-limited signal.** In octave bands C4's residual peaks at **62–125 Hz**, an octave below its own fundamental, and carries a second and far broader plateau from 250 Hz to 4 kHz about 15 dB under it; A2 does the same; C7's is flat from 31 Hz to 2 kHz. A shape fitted to the whole thing is a 100 Hz thump at every key — a sound the engine already makes twice over (the action's four events are centred at 77–300 Hz, and the board's own modes put a strike's low frequencies into every render).
