It is also why the correlation between the removed level and the *recording's* own `level` residual against its eight nearest same-`N` neighbours is only **r = +0.16** raw and **+0.22** detrended: had the fit been recovering a per-key level the piano has, that number would be near one. **One reading that is the listener's own note seen from the level side:** the recording's C4 sits at `level` z **+4.1** on its own compass — the recording, not the engine, is the outlier at the key the listener picked out of the melody, because the library's C4 take is 6.05 dB over the median of its neighbours and keys 59 and 61 are pitch-shifted copies of it, so the reference has a three-key plateau there that a fitted engine could only ever answer with a one-key spike. **What changed in the tree:** nothing rendered and no fitted table — `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, `renders/` and every gate are bit-identical, and the shipped `LEVEL_BAND_DB` stays 2.96 because the lifted control costs `D#4` a `centroid` flag at z +6.0 even though it costs the scoreboard nothing. Two comments were corrected to the controls above: `LEVEL_BAND_DB`'s, which claimed the 0.32 dB for itself, and `energy_offset`'s `notes.bridge_gain` refusal, which had the right verdict from three keys' values and now has the decomposition. **The gate stays red and the recommendation is to accept it**, on the ground that the only thing that moves it is the loudness leak item 272 convicted, that even paying it in full leaves the gate red at 4.45, and that the mean log-mel over six phrases cannot distinguish an instrument from a sampler that carries 2.26 dB rms of uncorrelated per-key take gain — which is a floor under that column and not a debt of the engine.

283. **The measurement tooling is now parallel and caches what is not under test; two findings fell out of doing it, and one of them is that the thing everybody assumed was expensive is not.** Every batch driver in `tuner/` rendered its notes one at a time on a fourteen-core machine, and the two gates a milestone is scored on cost a minute apiece. Three changes, all of them structural rather than numerical: **nothing that any of these tools asserts, prints or writes has changed**, and that is checked rather than asserted — `renders/compass/COMPASS.md` and `renders/realism/REALISM.md` are byte-for-byte the files the serial code wrote, and `cargo test --release --workspace` is the same single red (`a_known_duplex_comes_back_from_the_engines_own_render_of_it`, item 260's culling finding) with the same printed numbers to the last digit. **(a) The loops run across the cores.** `rayon` is a *dev*-dependency of `piano-tuner` — the library still measures one signal at a time and stays single-threaded; only the drivers that run it in bulk get a thread pool. Every parallel loop collects into an ordered container, and where a loop used to print as it went, the text is now built and emitted afterwards in key order, so console output and files alike are the same at any thread count. `compass_scan` **38.9 s -> 4.2 s**; `realism_bench` **59.0 s -> 14.3 s**; `tuner/tests/calibration.rs` **161.4 s -> 40.8 s**, which is thirteen of its twenty-one tests parallelised inside themselves — the gate's tests already ran concurrently with each other, so the wall time *was* one test, `a_known_comb_floor_comes_back_off_the_line_the_engine_draws` at **161.6 s of the 161.4**, five probes of eight layers of a 26 s A1 taken one after another. **(b) The reference side is cached to disk**, under `data/cache/reference/`, keyed by content: `sampler::SAMPLER_VERSION` (a new constant, hand-bumped, with the list of changes that require it on its doc comment), the SFZ file's bytes, and the material asked for. The reference is what the engine is *scored against* and it does not move when the engine does, so an iteration on the engine should not pay for it: `compass_scan` **4.2 -> 3.1 s** and `realism_bench` **14.3 -> 6.2 s** on a warm cache. A hit is bit-identical to a miss rather than close — f32 WAV is lossless and `tuner/tests/reference_cache.rs` renders one phrase both ways and compares bit patterns. **(c) The self-calibration gate keeps a corpus of tracked notes** under `data/cache/calibration/`, keyed on the preset TOML, the note, the tracker settings, and — this is the part that makes it safe — **a fingerprint of the engine's own audio and the tracker's own output on a probe note**, rendered through the gate's own `render_note`. There is no version constant to remember: any change to the engine's sounding path, to `presets/default.toml`, to the test's un-panning or to the tuner's transform or association moves the probe and misses the whole corpus. Second run of the gate: **40.8 s -> 35.6 s**, against a brief that asked for half of 161 s. **The finding is that (c) is worth a tenth of what it looks like it should be, and `pipeline.rs` said the opposite for years.** `analyze_trajectories`'s doc comment called it "the half of `analyze_note` that costs milliseconds", which is why a cached tracking pass looks like the whole answer. Measured on A1 held for 26 s, the note the gate leans on hardest: the **render costs 0.32 s**, `track_refined` **0.20 s**, and **the fits 2.5-3.7 s** — `fit_decays` over eighty partials of a twenty-six second record is 86 % of the cost, and it is the estimator under test, so it can never be cached without gutting the gate. The comment is corrected in place. So the corpus buys the 0.5 s of render-and-track per note and no more, which is the 5 s the numbers above show, and the brief's target was met by (a) alone. **A JSON corpus was built first and thrown away, measured:** `NoteTrajectories` is already `serde`-serializable and the obvious thing is to reuse it, but a note is 4.9 MB of pretty-printed digits and `serde_json`'s exact (`float_roundtrip`) parser costs about as much to read one as the tracker costs to recompute it — second run **36.1 s against the first run's 38.3 s**, i.e. nothing. The corpus is now a hand-rolled encoding of the bit patterns (`impl Cacheable for NoteTrajectories`), 59 MB against 239 MB and a `memcpy` to read; its encoder **destructures the struct exhaustively on purpose**, so a field added to `NoteTrajectories` is a compile error rather than a field silently dropped from every cached note, and `tests/reference_cache.rs` round-trips a signed zero, a subnormal and every truncation of a good file. **(d) The reference player was not reproducible, and had not been.** `Sampler`'s sustain-pedal-up and `AllOff` released the sounding keys in a `HashMap`'s iteration order; releasing a key appends its voices to the list that is summed into the output, floating-point addition is not associative, and `HashMap`'s order is reseeded per process. So two runs of `realism_bench` in the same tree wrote reference audio for the two pedalled phrases — `chords_pedal` and `excerpt` — differing at **58 675 and 115 198 samples, all by exactly one ulp**, with the other four phrases bit-identical. **No number in `REALISM.md` moved by a printed digit**, which is exactly why it survived: it was invisible to every gate and would have been invisible forever, except that a cache cannot be keyed on something that is not a function of its inputs. Releases are now in keyboard order. This *is* a change to rendered bytes — the two phrases' reference WAVs move by an ulp — and it is the only rendered change in this item. **What was rejected.** Caching the *engine* side of either driver: it is the thing under test, it is cheap, and a cache in front of it is exactly the way a stale measurement gets published. Caching `analyze_trajectories`: same reason, and it is the 86 %. Putting `rayon` in the library rather than in dev-dependencies to parallelise `fit_decays`'s eighty partials: it would shorten the gate's remaining critical path, but it makes every downstream user of the crate multi-threaded to speed up a test, and the gate's total CPU is unchanged by it — the tests already saturate the machine, so the wall time is CPU-bound at **327 s of work over fourteen cores**, and moving parallelism inside the fits moves no work. A `--refresh` flag on either cache: a cache that can be told to lie is a cache that will, and content keying makes the flag unnecessary. **Everything is checked for determinism rather than argued to be deterministic:** two full runs of the compass, of the realism bench (cold and warm cache, and two cold runs against each other) and of the calibration gate produce identical files and identical measured output, and the caches change nothing — deleting `data/cache/` is a slower run and the same numbers. Test count goes **497/1 to 505/1**: eight new tests, three unit tests of the fingerprint and five in `tuner/tests/reference_cache.rs`, and the one failure is the same documented duplex red. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean with and without `--features piano-tuner/diagnostics`.

284. **The seam was the defect and the fitted note was the correct one, so the 60 keys with no measured row are given texture drawn from the 28 that have one.** `tuner/src/estimate/texture.rs`, a fifth stage in `tuner/examples/fit_motion.rs` (`--stage texture`), and `notes.synthesized_texture` in both preset schemas. Item 274's closing line — "the 58 unfitted keys are not extended and cannot be" — is right about the thing it is about, that no value here may be a *measurement* of a key nobody recorded, and wrong about the conclusion: what a listener hears at C4 is not C4, it is the two keys either side of it carrying **zero** `partial_gains` cells and **zero** `false_beat` rows where C4 carries 16 and 5, and rendering 5.78 dB smoother than the piano where C4 renders 1.65 dB from it. The distributions those 28 keys measured are statements about the *instrument*; drawing from them is neither a measurement of an unsampled key nor a copy of its neighbour, and three rules keep it that way — no cell is ever read from another key's row (`TUNING_REPORT.md` §3's own finding, that the roughness is not shared between notes at the same frequency, refuses interpolation exactly as it refused a global bridge curve), the draw is conditioned on register and on nothing else, and it is seeded from the key number and one named constant (`TEXTURE_SEED`) so that a re-emitted preset is the same preset. Re-running the stage on its own output is **byte-identical**, because it clears every key `notes.synthesized_texture` names before it fits anything.

285. **The distributions, all fitted from the shipped preset and none of them chosen.** Quoted as `exp(a + b·key)` with the scatter its own keys had about that line. **Cells per row** `exp(5.058 − 0.03793·key)`, ×1.20, r **−0.981** — the line reads 71 at A0, 16 at C4 and 3 at F#7, and a drawn row is clipped to `MAX_ROW_CELLS = 48` and to the key's own bank, which is what the fitted bass rows are too (46-48 cells each). It is the *recording's* reach up the series and not the bank's: C4's bank has 80 partials and the tracker measured 16. **Roughness amount**, the `1.4826 MAD` of a row's cells about its own degree-2 curve in `ln k`: `exp(1.461 + 0.00089·key)` dB, ×1.43, r **+0.041** — 4.39 dB at A0, 4.55 at C4, 4.72 at F#7, i.e. **register-free**, and the model says so rather than fitting a curve to noise. Fitted from the 19 rows of at least 8 cells; the nine short treble rows cannot separate a curve from their roughness (a quadratic through four points in `ln k` goes through them, and their detrended MAD is exactly 0.00), and the two halves agree anyway — 4.40 dB median detrended over the long rows against 4.31 dB median *raw* over the short ones, which is what licenses one distribution for the whole compass. **Adjacent-partial correlation** lag-1 **+0.110**, Fisher-pooled over those 19 rows (mean +0.101, median +0.109, sd 0.238): all but white, with a slight positive tie, and it is drawn as a stationary AR(1) because `irregular` is precisely the statistic the difference between a tied and an untied row lands in. **The ceiling** — the recording's own `irregular` by register, which is what a row is trimmed against — is the one quantity that bends: `exp(3.476 − 0.06588·key + 0.0006793·key²)`, ×1.23, **R² 0.880** against a straight line's 0.520 and ×1.51, giving 10.9 dB at A0, 7.2 at C4, 45.8 at F#7. It is *validated out of sample*: the 60 keys it was **not** fitted from have recordings whose own `irregular` sits **+0.100 in ln** of that curve with a scatter of ×1.31, so a curve drawn through every third key describes the ones between. **Splits:** P(a key has any) **0.80** at or under key 94 and **zero above** — 20 of the 25 eligible fitted keys wrote one, and 0 of the 3 above, which is item 233's falsification refusing the treble and not a coverage failure; count given any `exp(1.980 − 0.01495·key)`, ×1.69, r −0.531 (5.3 at A0, 3.0 at C4, 1.8 at A6), clamped to 1..8; which partials by the fitted rows' own occupancy (k1 0.18, k2 0.17, k3 0.10, k4 0.11, k5 0.11, k6 0.13, k7 0.10, k8 0.10). **Rate:** one lognormal for the whole compass, `ln`-mean **+0.017**, `ln`-sd **0.584** (median 1.02 Hz), truncated to 0.370–3.00 Hz, which is the record's own floor (`FalseBeatLoop::MIN_FITTED_HZ`, one cycle in the window a rate is counted over) and the schema's ceiling. That single distribution is what preserves the two properties item 233 requires and this milestone was told to keep exactly: the measured `r(k, rate)` **within a key** is **+0.061** and `r(key, rate)` across keys is **+0.089**, so the rates know neither the partial number nor the register, and drawing them i.i.d. of both reproduces that by construction — measured back off the draw, the within-key correlation is inside ±0.2. **Depth:** `db = −46.44 + 0.3387·key + 2.076·k`, residual sd **10.55 dB**, R² 0.251, truncated to the schema's −40..0 — the two slopes are the only structure in it, and 75 % of it is scatter, which is why it is drawn and not predicted.

286. **What is not drawn, and the measurement that refuses it.** A fitted row is a **tilt** — a degree-2 curve in `ln k`, item 231's 7.5 dB at C4, the engine's own error in its smooth envelope — plus a roughness about it, and only the roughness is drawn. The tilt is a per-key **colour** that `compass_scan`'s `centroid` reads directly, and over the 28 fitted keys it is white down the compass: its value at `k = 1` has lag-1 autocorrelation **+0.45** between keys three semitones apart, at `k = 2` **+0.06** and at `k = 4` **−0.02**, and a degree-5 polynomial in key explains **39 %, 25 % and 26 %** of it. So it cannot be interpolated — the same decomposition that refused `notes.bridge_gain` the removed level in item 282 — and *drawing* it would write 6.6 dB of standard deviation of invented colour into 60 keys, which is the one thing an open `centroid` family (item 280) must not be given. The drawn row is therefore projected orthogonal to `1`, `ln k` and `ln² k` before it is written, which is not only the refusal but also what makes the trim work: `flatten_row` scales a row's departures from its own degree-2 curve, and a row with an accidental curve in it would have part of the draw read as tilt and left unscaled. **And the refusal has a corollary about length that was found the hard way.** A quadratic in `ln k` goes *through* three points, so a three-cell row has no roughness in it at all — it **is** its own tilt. Projected and then rescaled to the drawn amount, such a row is the arithmetic's own rounding, 1e-16 of it, multiplied by 1e16 and standing at the rails: measured on the first emission, C7-C8's rows came out as sign patterns of dust clipped to ±6 dB. So a drawn row is written only where it can be a roughness, `MIN_DRAWN_CELLS = envelope_degree + 2 = 4` (eight keys of the top octave draw fewer and get nothing), and a row between four and eight cells has only its **level** projected out, for the same reason `shaping::write_row` writes such a row's cells as measured. The fitted three-cell rows are kept, because they are measurements of *something*; a drawn one would be an invented colour with no roughness under it. The cost of the dust bug is worth recording because it is a warning about the compass rather than about this module: the dust rows scored **3 flags** where the correct rows score 7, all of the difference being the top octave's `decay`, which the halo makes a lottery (below).

287. **The drawn row is written under the fitted row's own discipline, with one deliberate difference and one refusal.** Same order as items 272-273 and, where it is a rule rather than a measurement, the same function: railed to its **own** spread (`shaping::own_rail`, split out of `rail_cells` so the two cannot drift apart — `3 × 1.4826 MAD`, floored at 6 dB, capped by the schema), pinned on **power** against the engine's own rendered spectrum (`shaping::energy_offset`, unchanged), and then closed on the **render** by `fit_motion`'s own `close_on_the_render` with the drawn ceiling in place of the recording's. **The difference is the level band.** `LEVEL_BAND_DB = 2.96` is the width of the piano's own key-to-key level scatter and what it licenses is a *measured* row carrying the part of a key's level the piano itself has; a drawn row has measured nothing about this key's level and gets `DRAWN_LEVEL_BAND_DB = 0.0`. It is free — item 282 measured the fitted rows at a zero band and it cost 0.00 dB of mel and moved no flag — and it is worth 0.03 dB of the scoreboard and takes the compass's mean per-key level move from −0.33 dB (sd 0.96, worst −3.30) to **−0.08 dB (sd 0.36)**. **The refusal is `ClosedRow::reached`.** Where even a row flattened to its own tilt renders rougher than the recordings of its register, a *measured* row is kept — it is evidence, and F#7 is the compass's own example (item 280) — and a **drawn** one is thrown away, because a draw that cannot be brought under the ceiling is not evidence of anything. Four of the 60 keys (43, 49, 50, 62) are refused that way and keep an empty row; without the rule, key 50 rendered `irregular` **11.83 against a ceiling of 5.15 and a recording of 5.4**. With the eight the length rule refuses, **48 of the 60 keys carry a drawn gain row** and 35 carry drawn splits. The drawn splits pass item 233's falsification too, applied to the draw and not assumed of it: eight rates drawn independently of `k` can still happen to rise with `k`, and a key whose drawn rates fail `RSS(flat)/RSS(∝k)` is redrawn up to 16 times and then written empty — which is exactly what `fit_false_beat` does to a key that fails it.

288. **The seam statistic, defined and measured.** Per-key rendered `irregular` down the compass must have no fitted/unfitted step. Two forms, both quoted because they answer different questions. **S1, the group difference in distance-from-its-own-recording:** mean over fitted keys of (engine − recording) minus the same over unfitted keys. Before `shaping.rs`'s own disciplined refit that was about **9 dB** (the fitted keys 4 dB *rougher* than their recordings and the unfitted 5 dB *smoother*, `temper`'s header); at the head of this milestone **2.33 dB** over all 88 keys and **3.23 dB** over A0-C6, where the recording's own `irregular` is a measurement rather than an analysis-floor reading; **after, 1.25 and 1.62 dB**. The bar is the recording's own key-to-key scatter — the mean absolute step in its `irregular` between neighbouring keys, **3.23 dB over the compass and 2.01 dB over A0-C6** — and both forms are now inside it. **S2, the raw step with the register trend removed:** the mean residual of rendered `irregular` about a degree-3 polynomial in key, fitted keys minus unfitted, **1.72 dB → 0.48 dB**. In A0-C6 the unfitted keys' distance from their own recordings falls from **4.67 dB to 3.44** where the fitted keys sit at 1.65, and their signed error from −4.56 dB to **−2.95** against the fitted keys' −1.33: the drawn keys are still the *smoother* half, so nothing here is over-textured. **C4 against the melody's own neighbours (57, 62, 64, 65, 67):** C4 renders 6.40 dB of `irregular` and its neighbours 3.80 / 6.50 / 6.60 / 6.00 / 8.40, so C4 stands **+0.14 dB over the neighbourhood mean** where it stood +1.20 before and where the recording has it **−2.74 dB under**, against a neighbour spread of ±2.90 dB on the recording — inside the piano's own contrast at that point in the phrase, from either side.

289. **The compass: 16 flags over 13 keys becomes 7 over 7, six of the seven were on the board before, and the seventh is a borderline `beat`.** `renders/compass/COMPASS.md` rescanned on the finished preset. The seven, worst first: `B6` `match` **+5.4** (was +5.1), `C5` `jitter` **+4.8** (was +5.8), `C7` `jitter` **+4.5** (was +5.3), `F7` `decay` **+4.5** (was +5.3), `E3` `beat` **+4.0** (**new**; E3 was worst at `centroid` 3.3), `A2` `centroid` **−4.0** (was −6.2) and `G7` `decay` **+4.0** (was +4.4). What cleared: `C5` `centroid` +6.3, `A2` `jitter` +5.3, `F#2` `centroid` −5.7, `C3` `beat` +5.6, `B6` `jitter` +4.6, `E5` `jitter` −4.4, **`F#7` `irregular` +4.2** — the last family-3 survivor, which cleared because it was never the row (item 280: F#7 renders 27.5 against a recording of 43.5, i.e. *smoother* than the piano) and its unsampled neighbours have now caught up with it — `F#5` `jitter` +4.2, `F3` `beat` −4.1 and `A1` `centroid` −4.0. The yardstick is not what moved: the worst `|z|` anywhere falls **6.3 → 5.4** and the mean of every key's own worst `z` falls **2.39 → 2.03**. **The one new flag, attributed.** E3 draws four splits and renders `beat` **10.1 dB against its own recording's 3.3**, the largest overshoot the drawn splits produce anywhere and the only one over +4.3; the recording's own E3 is itself an outlier the other way (`beat` z **−3.7** on the recordings' compass), so what E3 is being scored against is a neighbourhood the piano does not sit in either. The mechanism is the one thing in this milestone that is drawn *without* being closed on the render: a fitted key's split depth is bisected by `FalseBeatLoop` until the **rendered** beat depth is the recording's, and a drawn key's is taken straight from the fitted asks' own distribution, whose scatter is 10.55 dB against an R² of 0.251. Closing the drawn splits on a register-conditional beat-depth ceiling, exactly as the drawn rows are closed on a roughness ceiling, is the obvious next step and is not done here. Over the drawn keys as a group the splits *improve* the match: mean `beat` 11.05 → 11.42 dB against their recordings' 10.40, and mean distance from those recordings **2.62 → 2.50 dB**. **The top octave's `decay` is a lottery and this is where that was found.** `F7` and `G7` were already on the board (items 276, 280) as the tuner's own fitted-`sigma` step; what is new is how far they and their neighbours move when the *drawn* rows change. F#7 is a **fitted** key whose row nobody touched, and between two different treble draws its rendered `decay` moved **−15.9 → −12.4 dB/s** — through the sympathetic halo, since `partial_gains` multiplies a modal input gain and the undamped strings above G6 now answer the bridge with whatever texture they were given. One draw flags F#7 `decay` at +5.3, another flags G7 at +4.0 and clears F#7. That is a real coupling and not a bug, and it is a reason to distrust that family rather than to tune the seed. **The 28 fitted keys are untouched**: `level`, `centroid` and `irregular` are identical to the printed digit at every one of them, and only the halo-fed `beat`/`jitter`/`decay` of a few treble keys move a last digit. **And one column moves the wrong way, which is item 290's finding seen on the compass**: `match` — the mean absolute per-partial distance from a key's own recording, common offset removed — rises on the drawn keys from **10.70 to 11.13 dB** (8.60 → 9.06 over A0-C6) while the fitted keys' 7.17 does not move, and B6's flag goes +5.1 → +5.4. That is the same arithmetic as the scoreboard's: a per-partial absolute distance is minimised by the *smooth* engine, and texture that is right in distribution and wrong in detail is further from the reference than no texture at all. `irregular` and `match` are pointing in opposite directions on the same keys, which is exactly the disagreement item 270 opened this line of work on.

290. **The realism board goes 4.70 → 4.86 mean log-mel, the regression is 0.01 dB past the standing allowance, and the control says it is a property of the metric rather than of the draw.** Phrases 4.54 / 5.28 / 3.59 / 5.10 / 6.22 / 4.44 against 4.46 / 5.00 / 3.51 / 5.07 / 5.85 / 4.29; `modulation` 2.84 → 2.94; `attack` **3.56 → 3.57**, and `r bass` moves the *right* way (0.882 → 0.883) while `r mid` and `r treble` are unmoved to the digit. Motion columns hold at **A1 1.38 / A2 0.87 / B1 1.66 dB / B2 1.049** against 2.0 / 0.5 / 3 / 0.25 and against the 1.375 / 0.866 / 1.643 / 1.050 item 280 measured — the hundredths are the halo again, since Column B's own cells are keys 45, 60, 69 and 84 and every string around them is newly textured. **The brief's premise is that the reference at an unsampled key is transposed *real* texture, so texture should help; that premise is wrong for a mean-absolute distance, and the control measures it.** `fit_motion --stage texture --draw-over-measured` draws over the **28 measured** keys and leaves the other 60 bare — the shipped instrument with the same rows *drawn* instead of fitted — and the board reads **5.03**, against 4.70 with those rows measured and **4.98 with no rows at all** (item 274). So the same amount of texture, on the same keys, under the same discipline, is worth −0.28 dB when it is correlated with the reference and **+0.05 dB when it is not** — drawn texture is *worse than no texture* on this column. The arithmetic is the whole of it: for a reference whose roughness is `r` and an engine whose roughness is an independent `r'` of the same size, `E|r − r'| = √2 · E|r|`, so a metric that averages `|Δ dB|` per band is minimised by the smooth engine and charges 41 % of the texture's own contribution for getting the distribution right and the details wrong. From the none→measured step that contribution is 0.28 dB over 28 keys, which predicts +0.12 for none→drawn against the +0.05 measured (the mel bands average several partials, so they see less of it than the compass does); over 60 keys the same coefficient predicts the +0.17 that was measured. **This is item 270's argument in a new place**: the scoreboard is a mean over the compass, the defect a listener finds is not, and here the two point in opposite directions — the compass goes 16 flags to 7 and the seam 3.23 dB to 1.62 while the mel mean rises 0.17. **The recommendation is to accept the 0.01 and re-baseline at 4.86**, on the ground that the only way to buy it back is to remove the texture, that the drawn keys are still 1.6 dB *smoother* than their own recordings after it (−2.95 dB against the fitted keys' −1.33), and that a mean absolute log-mel distance cannot in principle reward a texture that is right in distribution and wrong in detail. The board's own noise floor — the same metric between two recordings of the same piano, 1.23-1.93 dB per phrase — is where an instrument that had the piano's texture statistically but not identically would sit, and it is 8 to 12 times the size of this move.

291. **Provenance is a field and not a comment.** `notes.synthesized_texture`, a strictly-ascending list of the MIDI keys whose `partial_gains` and `false_beat` rows were drawn, in both `engine::preset::NoteTables` and the tuner's mirror of it, validated on both sides (real keys, in order, each named once) and absent from a file that has none. A comment was the obvious alternative and does not survive the round trip through `serde` that every emitted preset makes; a naming convention inside the tables would have to be a magic value. The engine reads nothing here — a drawn row plays exactly like a measured one, which is the point of drawing it — and what it buys is that a library which later samples one of these keys can replace its rows without guessing which rows were measurements, and that the re-fit is idempotent by clearing exactly the keys the list names — measured, not asserted: a second run of the stage on its own output is **byte-identical**. **60 keys drawn for and 51 named**: the 58 the library never sampled, plus **A7 and C8**, which it did sample and which measured nothing (item 274) and so have no row to keep either; nine of them end up carrying nothing at all — D3, whose gain row its own ceiling refused and whose wire drew no split, and the eight keys of the top octave whose draw came out under `MIN_DRAWN_CELLS` — so they carry no drawn number and declare none. The field says which *rows* are drawn, and an empty row is not one; dropping them costs the idempotence nothing, because there is nothing at those keys for the clearing step to clear. **48 drawn gain rows and 35 keys with drawn splits**, against 28 measured gain rows and 20 measured split rows. **Also in this item:** `write_false_beats` now *merges* rather than replacing the whole table, because a stage that runs over the 30 sampled keys must not empty the 60 it does not visit; `MAX_GAIN_PARTIALS` moves into `estimate::shaping` as `MAX_ROW_CELLS`, since the fit and the draw are bounded by the same number; and `close_on_the_render` takes the ceiling as a number rather than as a `Series`, since a drawn key has no recording to take one from. **Nine new tests**: eight in `estimate::texture` — determinism, the drawn amount landing on the amount drawn, the draws landing inside the fitted distributions, the treble drawing no split while the rest pass the falsification, the drawn rates not knowing the partial number, the AR(1) sequence having the correlation it was asked for, the quadratic recovering one it is given, and `a_short_row_carries_its_draw_and_not_the_arithmetics_dust`, which is the regression test for item 286's finding — and `the_measured_presets_provenance_list_matches_its_own_tables`, which checks the list against the shipped preset's own rows in both directions. Eight more breakages join the two crates' shared refusal lists. `cargo test --release --workspace` is **514 green, 1 red — the same documented duplex one**, and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean with and without `--features piano-tuner/diagnostics`. **Not re-rendered and now stale by more than a constant: `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/` and `renders/salamander-ab/`**, for item 281's reason — each tool writes its audio and its report in one pass — and `renders/demo.wav` and `renders/scale.wav`, which are 60 keys' texture out of date. `renders/compass/` and `renders/realism/` are the two this milestone is scored on and both are regenerated.

292. **Brilliance is an absolute-frequency quantity and nothing in `renders/` measured one, which is why the listening note had no number under it.** `COMPASS.md`'s `centroid` is the power-weighted mean *partial index*: register-relative by construction, so two keys an octave apart with identical `centroid` differ by an octave in the band the ear calls air. `estimate::brilliance` and `examples/brilliance.rs` measure what the ear does instead — energy in fixed bands, engine against the recording of the same note, with the broadband level divided out, `HF(band) = 10 log10(E_band(e)/E_band(r)) − 10 log10(E_full(e)/E_full(r))` — because the two sides' levels are 13-22 dB apart across the compass and any statistic that did not remove that would be measuring `OUTPUT_GAIN`. Two bands, **2-6 kHz** (the top of the fitted partial series) and **6-12 kHz** (above every fitted mechanism on most keys); two instants, **0.1 s** and **1 s**, because a model can be right at one and wrong at the other for different reasons. Every number is quoted against the reference's own **velocity-layer spread** — the same key out of the recording layer next door, `realism.rs`'s device applied per key — which reads 1.17 / 2.69 dB over the compass and is what a finding has to beat. **What the audit says, per register, at 0.1 s (2-6 kHz / 6-12 kHz, floor beside it):** bass A0-B2 −2.55 / −2.10 (2.20 / 3.89), tenor C3-B4 **−5.47 / −8.84** (0.99 / 2.65), treble C5-B5 +1.77 / +4.27 (0.94 / 2.73), top C6-C8 −1.90 / **+15.16** (0.36 / 1.42). On the six phrases, 2-6 kHz reads −3.81 / +3.58 / −4.50 / −1.78 / +3.46 / −7.99 against a floor of 0.65, and the tilt over 500 Hz-8 kHz is **+0.36 dB/octave** against a floor of 0.34 — so there is no global darkness at all, and the deficit is *register-local*: the three phrases that stay under E4 (`scale_mf`, `chords_pedal`, `excerpt`) are 3.8-8.0 dB dark and the three that reach C6 and above are 3.5 dB bright. The listener's melody lives in the first register, which is why the note was "slightly" and why the mean never showed it.

293. **The top octave's decay is not short, and the measurement that said it was was reading the room.** `top_octave.rs` reports the engine 60 dB down at 1.26-1.82 s at A7-C8 where the recordings take 3.40-3.72, and that comparison is **broadband**. Measured on the partial instead — a heterodyne band-pass a quarter of `f0` wide, and a T60 *fitted* through the envelope in dB rather than read off a crossing, because a crossing is what a beat null does (item 46) — the engine's fundamental at those five keys lasts **1.80-2.33 s against the recording's 0.80-1.60**, i.e. **1.5 to 2.9 times too long**, and the same holds over the whole top register (median 3.45 s against 1.60) and the tenor (4.20 against 2.57). What the broadband statistic was crossing is the recording's own **floor**: at those keys the narrowband floor sits **72-92 dB** under the note's peak while the recording's late *broadband* level is only ~58 dB under it, so the late energy is room and neighbouring strings and not the struck note. `fitted_t60` refuses to fit under `FLOOR_MARGIN_DB` over that floor for exactly this reason, and the refusal is the finding: a note inside its own floor has an unmeasurable decay, not a long one. **Suspect (a) is refused, and fitting the top octave's decay targets to those recordings would have made the engine's top octave three times too long instead of twice.**

294. **The master shelf is acquitted on its own leverage, and the acquittal is a sweep rather than an argument.** `[soundboard].shelf_gain_db = -4.0` above 4 kHz has been hand-set since item 22 and never measured. Swept over its whole plausible range with `examples/brilliance.rs`'s in-memory override — 0, −2, −4, −6 dB — the phrase set's 2-6 kHz ratio moves **−1.15 / −1.55 / −1.84 / −2.06** and its 6-12 kHz **+2.83 / +1.81 / +0.93 / +0.19**. So the shelf's *total* authority over the audit is **0.9 dB in the lower band and 2.6 dB in the upper**, against register deficits of 5-9 dB and a phrase-to-phrase spread of 13 dB — and the two bands move in **opposite** directions, so no setting improves both: 0 dB buys 0.7 dB in 2-6 kHz and spends 1.9 dB in 6-12 kHz, taking the upper band from inside its 2.47 dB floor to outside it. The shipped −4 dB sits at the joint optimum to within the floor. Being a first-order shelf it is also the wrong *shape* for the defect twice over: it is one curve for all 88 keys where the error is −5.5 dB in the tenor and +1.8 dB in the treble, and it is a function of frequency where the error is a function of **partial number** — see 295.

295. **The error is in `k`, not in `f`, which convicts the hammer's envelope above the fitted rows and falsifies the fix that follows from it.** At the same absolute frequency, 8 kHz, the compass-summed level-matched difference at 0.1 s is **−9.01 dB in the tenor and +18.62 dB in the top octave**; at C4 that band is partial 30 and at C7 it is partial 4. No filter can be both, and the only thing that shapes a note's envelope in `k` above the reach its own `partial_gains` row was fitted to is the hammer pulse. That is suspect (c), and it is dominant. The fix it implies was built and measured before being believed: `estimate::brilliance`'s **envelope continuation** moves each key's partials above its measured reach by a line in `ln f` pinned to the two bands' corrections — `g = (W − (1 − s))/s` for the share `s` those partials actually carry, so the solve carries its own feasibility test and declines a band that is board or strike noise rather than string, which is what it does across the top octave — re-rendered and re-measured until it stops moving, six passes, smooth in `ln f` so it cannot buy brilliance by writing the jaggedness item 284 spent itself removing. **It works on the compass and breaks the phrases.** Compass at 0.1 s: tenor 6-12 kHz **−8.84 → −1.72**, inside its 2.65 dB floor; bass 2-6 kHz −2.55 → −1.31, inside; mean absolute over the compass 5.28 → 4.61 and 10.35 → **6.89**. Phrase set: 6-12 kHz **+0.93 → +5.46** against a 2.47 dB floor, the 8 kHz octave +0.96 → +6.46, the tilt +0.36 → +1.34 — every one of them *out* of the floor it was in. The reason is in the third column of the audit and is the real finding of this milestone: over 0.1→1 s the engine's 2-6 kHz already decays **11.2 dB** less than the recording's in the tenor and its 6-12 kHz **15.3 dB** less, against a layer floor of 0.42 / 0.86 dB, so a continuation fitted to the strike carries its lift through a tail that is already far too long, and a phrase integrates the tail. **A brightness that is right at the strike and wrong for the next second is not a fix**, so nothing is written to the preset: `presets/salamander-c5.toml`, `COMPASS.md` and `REALISM.md` are untouched by this milestone and every standing gate is where item 291 left it. The convicted defect is the high partials' **decay**, and it is a re-fit of `notes.sigma1` and `notes.partial_sigma_scale` — which the 28 sampled keys carry and the other 60 do not, the same seam item 284 closed for the gains — and therefore a milestone of its own. The continuation stays behind `--trim` so that milestone starts from a working instrument rather than from this paragraph.

296. **The complaint that opened item 284 is now a test, and it is the first statistic in this repository that plays a tune.** `tuner/src/estimate/melody.rs` and `tuner/tests/melody.rs`. Everything items 284-291 were scored on is a **compass** statistic — 88 keys struck alone at one velocity, each against its own neighbours — or a **phrase** statistic, a mean log-mel distance over six pieces of music. Neither of them is what the listener did. The compass never plays a melody; a mean over a phrase moves by hundredths when one of its thirty notes is sour, which is item 270's argument and the reason `COMPASS.md` exists at all. So the milestone's own trigger had no gate, and a milestone whose trigger cannot be re-run is a milestone that cannot be regressed. **What is rendered** is `realism::excerpt`'s soprano **alone**: the same thirty notes at the same onsets at velocity 88, with the harmony and the pedal taken away — the pedal because a pedalled line rings every note into the next and a per-note window then measures the phrase rather than the note, the harmony because keys 36-55 would put their own texture into the window. Both are built from the same `realism::ODE_MELODY`, extracted out of `excerpt` as a `const` so the gate cannot end up testing a line the scoreboard does not play; the extraction moves no event, and `REALISM.md` re-rendered on top of itself is **byte-identical**, which is the proof rather than the claim. **Three numbers per note**, measured identically on both signals over the same 0.03-0.40 s of the same note: `roughness`, the `Series::irregularity` the drawn `partial_gains` rows write; `wobble`, the median over partials of the RMS of that partial's dB envelope about its own straight-line decay, which is what the drawn `false_beat` splits write; and `hf`, the 2-6 kHz share of the note's power, which is item 292's absolute-frequency brilliance. `wobble` is not `Motion::beat_depth_db` and cannot be — `motion.rs` measures over 0.3-3.0 s and a melody gives one note 0.4 s before the next strike — it is the same physical quantity read in the window a *tune* leaves, which is the window the complaint was made in. **Two measurement rules were found the hard way and both are load-bearing.** The sampler plays each recording from its own start and every recording begins with however much silence there was before the hammer, so that offset is a property of one *sample file* and in a phrase it differs from note to note: each note's strike is therefore found again inside ±30/120 ms by the largest rise in a 1 ms envelope — a rise and not a level, because a melody note is struck into the tail of the note before it and any threshold on level fires on that tail. And the two half-beat passing notes are not measured at all, because 0.20 s of held key would put a different frequency resolution into the same column; both of their pitches sound five more times at full length.

297. **What the gate asserts, and why it is not the engine line's own evenness.** A real piano's melody is not even either: on these recordings the five pitches read 7.33 / 6.69 / 7.44 / 8.13 / 9.57 dB of roughness, and an engine line *smoother* than that is precisely the defect item 284 was opened on — so a gate that rewarded smoothness would reward the disease, and the pre-284 line would have scored **better** than the shipped one (0.86 against 1.54). The gate is therefore the listener's act with the piano as its yardstick: the largest departure of any note from the engine line's own **Theil-Sen** register trend — the median of the ten pairwise slopes, because a least-squares line through five points partly absorbs the very outlier this is looking for and C4, the note the complaint was about, is an endpoint where it absorbs most — held against the *same statistic on the recordings*, and never under the same statistic on the recordings' **neighbouring velocity layer**, which is two recordings of one piano. The floor is not a formality: four of the five pitches are transpositions, since the library samples every minor third and D4/E4 come out of one recording while F4/G4 come out of another, so their scatter against each other is smaller than a real piano's would be. `ALLOWANCE = 1.25` is the only chosen number and the readings are not near it. Reported beside the gate but **not** gated is the seam, `error = engine − reference` per note and its departure from the line's median error — item 288's S1 restricted to the melody — because no bar measured off this line can be set honestly for it: the melody contains exactly one key whose texture was ever fitted to a recording.

298. **What it says: the roughness seam closes on the melody, the brightness seam does not, and one note now wobbles unlike the rest.** Measured with item 284 undone in memory (every key `notes.synthesized_texture` names loses its drawn row and its drawn splits, which is the instrument at the head of the milestone since the 28 fitted keys were never touched) against the shipped preset. **`roughness`: stands out 0.86 → 1.54 against a bar of 1.67, pass in both; seam 2.46 → 1.52.** The line's distance from its own recordings goes −1.56 / −0.13 / −1.99 / −3.06 / −4.45 dB before and −1.56 / −0.04 / −1.82 / −2.61 / −1.16 after, i.e. **G4 comes from 4.45 dB smoother than its recording to 1.16**, which is the seam closing exactly where the compass said it would, and the standout rises only because the engine line is no longer flatter than the piano's. **`hf`: stands out 2.44 → 1.16 against a bar of 6.53, pass in both; seam 6.77 → 8.39, which does not close and is not meant to.** C4 is the one note of the line with a fitted row and its own row is worth **6.59 dB of 2-6 kHz share** — the same key rendered with its row cleared reads −32.65 against −26.06 — while a drawn row is worth nothing there by construction, because item 286 projects every drawn row orthogonal to `1`, `ln k` and `ln² k` and refuses to draw the tilt. That refusal is right and this is its price, measured: **the drawn rows close the roughness seam and cannot close the brightness seam**, and the number they cannot close it by is the 6.6 dB of invented `centroid` item 286 declined to write. It belongs to the brilliance line of work (292-295), not to this one. **`wobble`: 0.54 → 1.28 against a bar of 0.26 — FAIL on both presets, and worse after.** It is left red rather than skipped or widened. Two things are stacked in it. The floor is that the engine's melody line already stands 0.52 dB off its own trend with **every** `false_beat` row on the compass cleared, where the recordings' worst note stands 0.21 dB off theirs: the coupled unison's own beating is more uneven from key to key than the piano's, and that predates all of this work. On top of it, F4 is a key this milestone drew splits for and they take the column to 1.28, **2.4 times the bare engine's** — clearing the drawn splits alone and nothing else takes F4 from 2.64 dB of wobble to 1.97 and the column from 1.28 back to 0.56. That is the same defect and the same key class as item 289's new E3 `beat` flag, and item 289 named it in its own words: split *depth* is the one quantity this milestone drew **without closing it on the render**, where a fitted key's is bisected until the rendered beat depth is the recording's. The fix — closing the drawn splits on a register-conditional beat-depth ceiling — is still not done, and there are now two independent measurements asking for it, one on the compass and one on a tune.

299. **Everything else is where items 291 and 295 left it, re-measured rather than assumed.** `renders/compass/COMPASS.md` and `renders/realism/REALISM.md` regenerated on the shipped preset are **byte-identical to the files those milestones wrote**, which is both the determinism claim and the regression: the compass is **7 flags over 7 keys** (B6 `match` +5.4, C5 `jitter` +4.8, C7 `jitter` +4.5, F7 `decay` +4.5, E3 `beat` +4.0, A2 `centroid` −4.0, G7 `decay` +4.0) against the 16-over-13 this line of work started from, and the board is mel **4.86**, `modulation` 2.94, `attack` 3.57, `r` 0.883 / 0.928 / 0.888, with the motion columns at **A1 1.38 (≤2.0), A2 0.87 (≥0.5), B1 1.66 dB (≤3), B2 1.049 (≥0.25)**. `renders/salamander-ab/` and `renders/demo.wav` — named stale by item 291 — are re-rendered on the finished preset and are new bytes, which is what a preset that moved 60 keys' texture should do. **Not re-rendered and still stale:** `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/` and `renders/scale.wav`, for item 281's reason. **Item 284's idempotence is re-measured and not taken on trust**: `fit_motion --stage texture` run again on the shipped preset writes a file that is **byte-identical** to it, reporting the same 60 keys drawn, 1015 gain cells, 129 split rows over 35 keys and 4 rows refused by their own ceiling, with the 28 measured keys' rows kept. `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **535 green, 2 red** — the documented duplex and the documented wobble — and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean with and without `--features piano-tuner/diagnostics`. The performance budget reads **29.4 % of one core** worst case, 30.3 % on the measured preset's duplex, 30.2 % with six segments on every key, against 50 %; the master limiter is never reached, the loudest single strike sitting **8.20 dB** under the threshold on the measured preset and 8.86 on the default. The one new cost is the gate's own: `cargo test -p piano-tuner --test melody` is **2.3 s** in the dev profile, three renders and 28 windowed notes, and its reference side is cached to disk under the same content key every other reference render uses.

300. **The one drawn quantity that was never closed on the render is now closed on it, against the recordings' own beat depth by register and partial.** `estimate::texture::BeatCeiling`, `TextureModel::beat_ceiling`, `SynthesizedTexture::beat_ceiling_db` and `fit_motion`'s `close_splits_on_the_render`. Items 289 and 298 named the same defect from two directions and left it: a fitted key's split depth is bisected by `FalseBeatLoop` until the **rendered** beat depth is the recording's, while a drawn key's was taken straight from `TextureModel::depth` — the fitted *asks*, residual sd **10.55 dB against an R² of 0.251**, so three quarters of what was written was scatter. A `notes.false_beat` row's `db` is a **request** (how far under the partial a companion sits) and nothing in a preset says how deeply the engine will then beat; that is a property of the key's own unison, its damping and the rate. **The ceiling is measured, not chosen**: the recordings' own `Motion::beat_depth_db` per key and partial, read with the same `measure` the fitted keys' targets are read with, over the 25 sampled keys at or under `HIGHEST_FALSE_BEAT_KEY` (above it item 233's falsification refuses the mechanism, so a treble key's beat is not evidence about it), fitted as `ln db = −1.825 + 0.04662·key + 0.1183·k` over **197 cells**, R² **0.539** against a key-only fit's 0.501 and a `k`-only fit's 0.028 — the register term is nearly all of what is explainable and the partial term is kept because it is the difference between 0.48 dB at A0 k1, **2.98 at C4 k1 and 6.81 at C4 k8**, which is a factor of 2.3 across a wire whether or not it moves an R². The scatter is ×2.29 and it is **decomposed rather than pooled**: ×1.99 between keys and ×1.70 within one, and both are drawn — one `z` for the key, one per cell, a fifth `Draw` stream so that adding it moved no cell of the four the gain rows come from. Drawing only the key's would refuse a whole key's splits on one unlucky number; drawing only the cell's would give every key the average wire. **The close** is the fitted keys' own shape: a baseline round with the key's splits cleared, then the draw itself, then six halvings of the bracket between the schema's quietest companion and the drawn ask, keeping the loudest ask whose render came in `BEAT_MARGIN_DB = 0.25` under the ceiling — a search over a quantity that is monotone in the level, which is why the endpoint is the answer here where `FalseBeatLoop` keeps its best visited point. The rate is never touched: it is drawn i.i.d. of `k` and of the register because that is what item 285 measured, it passes item 233's falsification as drawn, and there is no recording of this key to step it against. **Three verdicts, and the middle one is the point**: a draw already under its ceiling is written whole, because a draw is not a target and lifting every quiet key onto its ceiling is the seam with its sign reversed; a draw over it is trimmed; and a partial that beats over its ceiling with **no split at all** is refused, because a companion only adds and a draw that cannot be brought under the ceiling is not evidence of anything — `ClosedRow::reached`'s rule for rows, applied to splits, and `FalseBeatLoop`'s own `dropped` applied to a drawn key.

301. **What that costs in splits, and the control that says the cost is the instrument's and not the ceiling's.** Of **129 drawn asks, 54 are written** over 23 keys (against 129 over 35), **14 are trimmed** by 9.4 dB on average, **75 are refused** and **5 are kept unclosed** because the render did not resolve the partial — `FalseBeatLoop::observe`'s rule that nothing can be concluded from a partial that did not measure. A 58 % refusal rate is a large number and it is quoted against the one that makes it readable, which the stage now measures on every run: **with no split written at all, the engine's own unison already beats over the recording's own depth at 73 of 192 sampled cells (38 %, median −0.34 dB)** — those are exactly the cells a *fitted* key's loop drops, for exactly the same reason. The gap between 38 % and 58 % is not severity but independence: a fitted key's target is its own recording's depth at that cell and is correlated with what the engine does there, and a drawn key's ceiling is a draw that cannot be. Where it fires is where it should: the tenor's unsampled keys render 8-18 dB of beat at their upper partials with nothing written (G#4 k5 **14.8**, k8 **18.1**), and adding a wire's split to that is adding an artefact to a defect. The preset: 60 keys drawn for, **50 named**, **49 drawn gain rows and 1043 cells** (48 and 1015 before — one more row now passes its own roughness ceiling, because the row is closed on a key whose splits have already been closed), 3 rows refused by that ceiling, and the 28 measured keys untouched. `fit_motion --stage texture` run again on its own output is **byte-identical**, which is item 284's idempotence re-measured and not assumed.

302. **The boards: the defect goes, the roughness seam holds, the jitter seam closes, and three flags come back on a yardstick that contracted.** **The melody** (`renders/melody/MELODY.md`): `wobble` **1.28 → 0.43** against a bar of 0.26, with F4 — the key item 298 convicted — going from **2.64 dB to 1.54 against its recording's 1.98**; `roughness` 1.54 → **1.23** (bar 1.67, pass) and its seam 1.52 → 1.18; `hf` 1.16 → **1.60** (bar 6.53, pass). The column stays **red**, and the attribution is now measured from the other side rather than argued: clearing every drawn split from the shipped preset moves it 0.43 → **0.44**, so the drawn splits are worth **0.01 dB** of what is left, and clearing the measured ones too takes it to 0.26. The standout note is **D4**, which carries no drawn row, no drawn split and no measured one, and wobbles 2.21 dB against its recording's 1.95: the coupled unison's own key-to-key unevenness, which predates all of this and is not this milestone's to fix. **The compass** (`renders/compass/COMPASS.md`): **7 flags over 7 keys → 8 over 7**. `E3` `beat` **+4.0 clears** — the flag this fix was aimed at — with E3's rendered `beat` **10.13 → 5.2 against its recording's 3.3** and its `jitter` 0.66 → 0.26 against 0.28, its worst score now `centroid` 2.1; `A2` `centroid` −4.0 clears too. Three `jitter` flags return: `A2` +5.0, `B6` +4.5, `F#5` +4.2. **Every one of them reads the same numbers as before to the printed digit** — A2 1.15 against its recording's 1.21, F#5 2.12 against 2.17, B6 2.10 against 2.02 — and so do C5's 1.68 and its neighbourhood's 0.73 → 0.74 while its `z` goes 4.8 → 5.5. What moved is the yardstick: the compass's `z` is a residual over the **MAD of every residual the metric has**, and that MAD contracts by about a seventh when 75 splits leave the unsampled keys. The worst `|z|` anywhere goes 5.4 → 5.7 and the mean of every key's own worst `z` 2.03 → 2.13. **The quantities under the flags move the right way.** Over the drawn keys, rendered `beat` against their own recordings' goes **+0.62 → +0.30 dB** signed (mean absolute 2.451 → 2.442), and over the fitted keys +1.98 → +1.80 through the halo. `jitter` is the trade and it is small and it closes a seam: the drawn keys go −0.655 → **−0.704** signed against the fitted keys' −0.728 → **−0.706**, so both halves of the compass now sit the same 0.70 cents under the piano where the drawn half used to sit 0.07 closer to it than the sampled half — the engine's jitter deficit is global and is now uniform, which is what a seam statistic asks for and what `A2`'s flag is the local price of. The roughness seam item 288 defined is where it was: **S1 1.25 → 1.28** over the compass and 1.62 → **1.67** over A0-C6 against a bar of 3.23 / 2.01, **S2 0.48 → 0.51**. **The realism board** (`renders/realism/REALISM.md`): mel **4.86 → 4.87**, `modulation` 2.94, `attack` 3.57 → 3.55, `r` 0.883 → 0.885 / 0.928 / 0.888 → 0.886, and the motion columns unmoved to the digit at **A1 1.38 (≤2.0), A2 0.87 (≥0.5), B1 1.66 dB (≤3), B2 1.049 (≥0.25)**. One hundredth of mel is a fifth of the smallest quantity that board can resolve (its own noise floor is 1.23-1.93 dB per phrase) and it is inside item 290's standing allowance. **Re-rendered on the finished preset**: `renders/compass/`, `renders/realism/`, `renders/melody/`, `renders/demo.wav` and `renders/salamander-ab/`. **Still stale, for item 281's reason**: `renders/analysis/`, `renders/jitter/`, `renders/timbre-ladder/` and `renders/scale.wav`.

303. **The gates, re-run rather than assumed.** `cargo test --release --workspace --no-fail-fast` is **538 green, 2 red** — the documented duplex and the documented wobble, both named in their own files — against item 299's 535 and 2; the three new ones are `estimate::texture`'s `every_drawn_split_carries_a_ceiling_of_its_own` (the parallel arrays `close_splits_on_the_render` zips are a contract, not a convention), `the_drawn_ceilings_land_inside_the_fitted_distribution` (their median sits on the surface they were drawn from, their scatter is the fitted scatter, and more than half the keys draw a ceiling that varies between their own partials, which is the two-term draw doing what it is for) and `the_beat_ceiling_recovers_a_surface_it_is_given` (including that a depth of zero is dropped rather than floored). `engine/tests/preset.rs`'s provenance test moves from 51 named keys to **50** and says which key left and why: D4 now joins D3 and the top octave in carrying nothing, its gain row refused by its own roughness ceiling and both its splits by its own beat ceiling. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` is clean with and without `--features piano-tuner/diagnostics`. The performance budget reads **30.9 %** of one core worst case, 30.3 % on the measured preset's duplex and 30.6 % with six segments on every key, against 50 %; the master limiter is never reached, the loudest single strike sitting **8.20 dB** under the threshold on the measured preset and 8.86 on the default. The stage costs what it always did plus one render per drawn key with splits: `fit_motion --stage texture` over all 88 keys is **23 s** on an M4 Pro, of which the split closing is 129 asks over 35 keys at 8 renders each.
